They do not show a result received on the phone, BLE, or ARKit. Swift 6.1 is the minimum toolchain, because the pinned dependencies need tools version 6.1; the Mac needs Xcode with Swift 6.1 or newer.

**The C functions for Unity (`Sources/CommsBridge/DisplayMailbox.swift`).** The Week 7 build installed on 2026-09-28 (the same package under its earlier name `Week7Native`) had the first three only under their Week 7 names, `Week7Start`, `Week7CopyDisplay` and `Week7Stop`, which the patched export calls. The `Comms*` names (added 2026-10-08), `CommsNextResult` and the display-text and validation changes of 2026-10-14 are in the source and, since 2026-10-16, in the rebuilt Unity export, which replaced the Week 7 build on the owner's iPhone that afternoon (above). In the unsigned build of that export, `UnityFramework` defines `Week7Start`, `Week7CopyDisplay`, `Week7Stop`, `CommsStart`, `CommsCopyDisplay`, `CommsStop` and `CommsNextResult` (checked with `nm`), and the `Week7*` calls of the patched IL2CPP code (`libGameAssembly.a`) resolve to these wrappers; no `Week7*` or `Comms*` symbol is left undefined. Nothing in that export calls `CommsNextResult`: of the bridge functions, the patched IL2CPP code calls only the three `Week7*` ones (`nm -u` on `libGameAssembly.a`). The signed build installed on the iPhone at 13:13 was built from the same export and source; the one installed last adds the settings-save fix, which does not touch these functions.

| Function | Semantics |
|---|---|
| `void CommsStart(void)` | Installs the button and the foreground/background notifications. **Does not start a connection**; only the user tapping the button and entering passwords starts one. Call it once |
| `int32_t CommsCopyDisplay(char* buffer, int32_t capacity)` | Takes one snapshot and builds four lines of UTF-8 text. If the text differs from what was last delivered and fits, it writes the text into the buffer (with a terminating 0) and returns the byte count. Otherwise it returns 0 and writes nothing. It never returns a negative number |
| `void CommsStop(void)` | The status changes to `Stopped` at once; everything is removed and the passwords are cleared. Call it only when the app truly stops using Communications |
| `int32_t CommsNextResult(char* buffer, int32_t capacity)` | Takes out the oldest waiting result as one compact JSON object, `{"result_id":"…","gesture":"…","confidence":…,"device_id":…}`, with a terminating 0, and returns its byte count. Returns 0 when nothing waits. When the buffer is null or smaller than the bytes plus the 0, it writes nothing, keeps the result and returns minus (bytes + 1). Details in 7.3 |

The four lines of text (the source since 2026-10-14, built into the app installed on the owner's iPhone on 2026-10-16; the Week 7 build of 2026-09-28 that it replaced showed `Week 7 • DUMMY inference` on line 1 and `confidence 1.0` on line 3):

```text
Communications
<status>
<gesture>  |  <result_id>  |  confidence <the received confidence>        (when there is no new result, this line is No live result)
Received: <N>
```

Rules for the caller: only one poller; poll on the main thread every frame; use a 2048-byte buffer; decode as UTF-8; **use `result_id` to decide whether a result is new, not the label text**; do not call `CommsStop` on a scene change or when going to the background.

What the text can and cannot tell the game:

- The status `Subscribed` means only that the subscription handshake with the Ultra96 succeeded. It does not prove that results are flowing.
- Line 3 holds only the latest result, and the next result overwrites it. Results that arrive between two polls can be lost: if `CHOP` and then `PLACEDOWN` arrive between two frames, the game sees only `PLACEDOWN`. Comparing `result_id` stops the game acting twice on one result, but it cannot recover an overwritten one. `Received` still counts every result.
- So the text is a status display, not a game input. The game's actions are one-shot events, so the game needs results one at a time, not a display that the next result overwrites. That is `CommsNextResult` (7.3): each result that reaches the phone and is not dropped can be taken out at most once; it does not promise that every result is delivered. It is compiled and tested on Linux and macOS, built for iOS and installed on the owner's iPhone in the rebuilt Week 7 export (2026-10-16), which does not call it.

**What "Week 7 receiver" means exactly (confirmed by the owner):** the `CommsNative` Swift client on the iPhone (the copy installed on 2026-09-28 was built from this package under its earlier name, `Week7Native`; on 2026-10-16 a build from current source replaced it on the owner's iPhone, 6.3 step 4), plus the bridge in the Unity export that calls `Week7Start` / `Week7CopyDisplay` / `Week7Stop`. It is the receiver made temporarily for the Week 7 demo:

- The Unity source project was not available at the time, so it patches the generated code of one specific Unity export (`ios-visualizer/xcode-export/`, fork only). `ios-visualizer/tools/patch_export.py` rewrites four methods of Jie Ling's `TlsDataReceiver`: `Start` calls `Week7Start()`; `ListenLoop` is emptied, so the 5005 listener no longer runs; `Update` calls `Week7CopyDisplay(buffer, 2048)` every frame and writes the text into the label; `OnDestroy` calls `Week7Stop()`. `configure_xcode.py` links `CommsNative` into `UnityFramework`.
- The patch is locked to file hashes and accepts only that one export. It refuses an export made again from `vis/`.
- **It only displays text and has never triggered a game action.**
- **The `week7` names inside it are not touched**: the three `Week7*` wrapper functions, the old iOS data directory `Application Support/Week7` (read while the `Comms` files are missing, and since 2026-10-16 also written when `Comms` cannot be created; see above), the app display name `Week 7 Visualizer`, and the comments and temporary-file prefixes in the patch scripts.

**What is under `phone/` is for reference and diagnostics. It is not the receiver deployed on the iPhone:** `phone/receiver.py` (Python, Termux/iSH), `phone/unity/CommsPhoneCore.cs` and `CommsPhoneReceiver.cs` (Android reference; the phone must open its own SSH local forward to `127.0.0.1:19999`), and `phone/ish_control.py` (temporary maintenance script for the iSH route).

**Build status of the C# reference (2026-10-15): the core `CommsPhoneCore.cs` compiles, and its self-test passed (113 checks, PowerShell 7); the Unity component was checked by reading only.** `phone/unity/CommsPhoneCore.cs` compiled on its own at C# 7.0 with 0 errors and 0 warnings, against the .NET 10 libraries (not .NET Standard 2.1, which Unity uses). `pwsh -NoProfile -File phone/tests/run_core_tests.ps1` (PowerShell 7.6.7) passed 113 checks in 4 runs. No C# change was needed. `CommsPhoneReceiver.cs` (the Unity component) was checked by reading only. One behaviour differs from the iOS client: the Android reference empties its result queue at every new connection, automatic reconnects included (`phone/unity/CommsPhoneCore.cs:387` and `:426`), while the iOS queue keeps results across an automatic reconnect (they still expire after 2 s).

### 4.7 Evidence levels (none replaces another)

| Level | Evidence | Proves | Does not prove |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Source statistics on the board: `submitted` | The Bluetooth stack accepted the notification submission | That the laptop received it |
| 2 | Laptop counts. `callback_received` is the BLE callback count; the progress line prints it as `received=`. The report's per-board `received` counts packets the writer task dequeued and processed. The two differ when the queue drops packets | `callback_received`: the BLE callback got the bytes. `received`: the writer task took them from the queue | That the board received it |
| 3 | `INGEST_ACK` | The board service validated and accepted this input, and (for a stream sample) that the model's `on_sample` returned | That the phone received it; that the model produced a result |
| 3a | Board counters `model_samples` / `model_results` (the `stopped` line) and the events `model_result` / `model_result_refused` | How many samples went to the model and how many of its results the board accepted for sending | That a result reached the phone |
| 4 | Board event `result_write_complete` | The board wrote the result into the connection | That the phone validated or displayed it |
| 5 | `Received: N` on the phone screen (read by a person) | Within this connection the phone accepted N distinct results | Individual IDs; that a game action was triggered |

Since 2026-10-15 the level-1 source statistics also count the ticks that sent nothing (`idle`, `unread`, `refused`; 4.2). They show that the board kept ticking, not that anything was sent. The `CAPTURE PASSED` printed by `demo.py` is level 3. The phone sends no application-level acknowledgement and keeps no per-result record. For the phone level, a person compares the increase in `Received` with `Phone expected increase`; that comparison is valid only while the board runs the built-in dummy model (one result per accepted input). With a real model, the phone's increase is compared with the board's level-4 count, `result_write_complete`, for the run (6.5, D39); no tool tallies that from the board yet (13). `tools.check_board` reaches level 3 plus the results on a local subscriber, all on the laptop.

### 4.8 Trust material

| Material | Who holds it | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| CA private key `ca-key.pem` | Only the owner's laptop (outside the repository) | Never on the board, never on the phone |
| CA certificate `ca-cert.pem` | Laptop (`--ca`); iPhone (imported) | The current CA's SHA-256 is `4dfba4905c171e68c3623dbc952154149076ed89004b85d475e858cc550760ec`, valid until 2027-09-06. This fingerprint is pinned in `Configuration.swift`. The renewal of 2026-10-14 kept this CA, so the laptop's CA file (`~/.codex/private/cg4002-week7-20260906/ca-cert.pem`) and the CA imported on the phone stay valid |
| Server certificate and key | `tls/` in the Ultra96 deployment directory (`BOARD_ROOT/tls/server-cert.pem`, `server-key.pem`) | **Since 2026-10-16 the board holds the renewed pair** (same CA, CN/SAN `ultra96.week7.internal`, valid 2026-10-14 14:22:35 to 2026-11-13 14:27:35 UTC), installed by `tools.deploy_service --server-cert-dir` with the version-3 service (6.3 step 1). The deployment record reads `certificate_not_after: Nov 13 14:27:35 2026 GMT`, and the synthetic rehearsal's TLS verified it against the CA from the laptop (6.1); the owner's iPhone verified it against its imported CA when it subscribed later that day (4.6). The previous pair, with the September certificate that expired on 2026-10-06 (date read from the laptop's copy), is backed up in `BOARD_ROOT/tls/backup-20261016T050629Z/`. The source copy of the renewed pair stays in `~/.codex/private/cg4002-comms-server-20261014/` on the owner's laptop, outside the repository. Renew again before 2026-11-13 (9.2) |
| Both SSH host public keys | The laptop's `known_hosts`; the iPhone source | No trust on first use |
| SSH passwords | Only the operator | The laptop enters them at the OpenSSH prompt. The phone keeps them only in memory |
| BLE bond keys | One copy each on the ESP32 and on Windows | The passkey is shown only on the board's serial port |

### 4.9 Where documents and code disagree (the code wins)

- `vis/README.md` says "the phone runs a TLS server on port 5005". The direction is the opposite of the network model. In the Week 7 export this code has been emptied.
- The archived architecture draft says `stfjump`; the code and the site use `stujump`. Its example port is 8888; the code uses 18888 and 18889.
- In `docs/week7-runbook.md`, `tools.generate_week7_pki`, `tools.rehearse_week7` and `Week7Server` have since been renamed `tools.generate_comms_pki`, `tools.rehearse_comms` and `CommsServer`.
- The archived iOS design specification says "wait 30 s for the first result". The current code has no idle limit after subscribing.
- `docs/B07-CO-protocol-explained.zh-CN.md` explains protocol version 2 (32 bytes, 8 values, `LCS1`). The code is version 3; `docs/co-protocol-v3.md` governs.

---

## 5. Why it is designed this way

1. **Every connection dials out; board services bind only to loopback and are reached only through SSH.** Under the NUS firewall rules, the Ultra96 can be reached from outside only through the VPN and the jump host `stujump`, using SSH (port 22). The laptop and the phone are on no allow-list, so nobody can connect in to them. These approaches were therefore rejected: the phone as a server waiting for incoming connections (the teammate's original 5005 approach); the laptop connecting directly to the phone; opening the board's 8888/9999 to the outside; SSH reverse forwarding; the laptop forwarding results.
2. **The phone connects to the board itself, not through the laptop.** The phone owns its whole route, so results do not depend on the laptop being online. The laptop is only an input relay.
3. **TLS inside SSH, with a fixed host name.** SSH gets the connection there. TLS, with a dedicated CA and a fixed name, confirms that the connection reaches the right service, even though TCP connects to `127.0.0.1`. There are no mutual certificates, because SSH has already authenticated the client.
4. **No retransmission, no replay, bounded queues, 2 s freshness.** A stale gesture delivered late would be taken as a new game action. So every segment drops data rather than send old data. Queues are bounded, so memory cannot grow without limit during a fault.
5. **The `device_id` + `boot_id` + `seq` triple; the sequence number is assigned before submission; separate read-only source statistics.** After a board reboot the sequence restarts at zero without being confused with old data. A sample whose submission failed still uses a sequence number, so a loss is not hidden. Losses at the start and end can be reconciled.
6. **ACK window 32.** On the remote route a single ACK took up to 2.375 s. With stop-and-wait per frame, later samples exceeded the 2 s freshness and were dropped (two 600 s tests dropped 1 and 33). With window 32, a 600 s test had every sample acknowledged. The freshness limit itself was not relaxed.
7. **One independent channel per board.** When one board loses power or stalls, the other continues. Measured during a power-loss recovery: the healthy board had 111 packets and 111 ACKs.
8. **BLE uses Secure Connections + passkey + bond, with no extra application-layer encryption.** The authenticated BLE link plus the SSH/TLS after it covers the whole path. A home-made key protocol on top would only add something unreviewed.
9. **One subscriber; a newcomer replaces the old one.** V1 has only one phone, and the result stream always has exactly one owner.
10. **Length prefix + strict JSON; close the connection on error.** TCP has no message boundaries. After a bad frame there is no reliable way to resynchronise.
11. **Host public keys and the CA fingerprint are pinned in the phone source.** The phone has no step where a person checks `known_hosts`, so there is no trust on first use. The cost: changing the board or the CA means rebuilding the app.
12. **Passwords only in memory; leaving the foreground disconnects.** The route on site uses password authentication. Nothing is written to disk, so there is no credential file to manage. It also does not rely on unverified iOS background survival.
13. **One process on the board, standard library only, no sudo, no system service.** The board is shared; do not disturb other people's environment. The start script starts the service only when the ports are free and never stops or kills an existing process.
14. **Graded evidence.** ACKs and phone results use two independent connections, and there has been a case where all ACKs arrived while the phone received fewer results. So each evidence level speaks only for its own segment.
15. **BLE rather than Bluetooth Classic.** This choice was made and tested long ago: the 50 Hz and 70 Hz settings were both measured clean on 2026-09-28, with the 32-byte dummy packet (the 70 Hz setting actually delivered about 66 packets/s). With 38-byte packets, one board at 50 Hz was measured clean on 2026-10-14 and 2026-10-16; two boards together and real sensor data have not been measured.
16. **Only one protocol version at a time (the owner's rule, 2026-10-14).** Each component accepts only version 3, so there is no mixed state to reason about; an upgrade means reflashing both boards, redeploying the board service and reinstalling the phone app together. The source-statistics marker moved to `LCS3` so that a board with old firmware is refused at start-up with a clear message instead of failing packet by packet. The subscription envelopes stay at version 1 because they did not change.
17. **Communications checks the shape of a result, not its meaning.** Any label of 1 to 32 safe characters and any confidence from 0 to 1 passes; the label set is for Wen Ree and Jie Ling. The board refuses a result the phone would reject, because a rejected result ends the phone session and clears its passwords.
18. **The model runs behind a narrow, counted interface.** `on_sample` runs on the service's single event loop before the ACK, so it must return at once; slow calls and exceptions are counted rather than allowed to close connections. `emit` may be called from any thread and moves the result onto the event loop, because the result queue has no lock. Commands never reach the model: their values are transformed dummy rows and their sequence numbers come from the command space, so they would corrupt the model's windows. The built-in dummy model keeps one result per input, so every acceptance check stays valid while it runs; with a real model the phone level is checked against the board's `result_write_complete` count instead (6.5, D39).
19. **Deploying is one checked step with rollback.** The board is shared and has no service manager. `tools.deploy_service` checks the new code, the certificate and key, and the running process (this user's, under the deployment folder, owning exactly the two ports) before stopping anything, and restarts the previous code and certificate if the new service does not come up.
20. **The glove decides when to send; Communications calls the decision once per sample (2026-10-15).** Siqiang's answer is that a glove sends only while his detector says MOVING, and the owner asked that the Communications firmware call his function. Calling it right after each successful read keeps the state out of the packet: no new field, no change to the 38-byte layout, the MTU floor or `on_sample`. The cost is that samples before the MOVING decision are never sent: they cannot be sent later, because the link's ceiling (about 66 packets/s per board) leaves no room for a backlog. That is a property of the detector, for Wen Ree to judge (7.2).
21. **A tick that sends nothing takes no `seq`, but it is counted.** A `seq` gap keeps one meaning: a lost sample. A pause (IDLE, no valid reading, refused) shows only as an `uptime_ms` jump. The skipped ticks are counted in the source statistics (`idle`, `unread`, `refused`), so the laptop can still reconcile and can tell a still player from a stalled board. The statistics grew to 36 bytes but kept the marker `LCS3`: the 24-byte `LCS3` ran only on the right board, and the length change alone refuses older firmware with a message to reflash.
22. **A gated board is judged by its ticks.** Idle ticks count toward coverage, because the board kept ticking, and silence is not judged once idle ticks are reported. The expectation is taken over the time sensor notifications were on, the same interval the board's counters cover, so a board that subscribed before the other one is not judged over the wrong window.
23. **Ingest connections may stay silent for 600 s, and a full server evicts the longest-silent one.** With the old 5 s limit every pause longer than 5 s closed a gated glove's stream, and the first frames after the pause were lost as ambiguous during the TLS reconnect. Once a frame has begun, 5 s still bounds it. A longer limit lets a vanished laptop's connection hold one of the 8 slots for up to 600 s (sshd may keep its socket), so when the slots are full the connection that has waited longest (at least 5 s) is closed instead of refusing the new client.
24. **Glove module calls never run under the connection mutex.** A call may take 10 to 20 ms (Siqiang's answer C), and the BLE callbacks take the same mutex. The tick is re-checked under the mutex and committed only if the laptop is still ready, so the source statistics stay unchanged once notifications are off, which the shutdown check relies on. The send tick advances by whole intervals, so a slow call delays one tick without lowering the rate.
25. **Siqiang's module is built from unchanged copies.** His folder is read-only, and his include path resolves only in a PlatformIO library layout. A tool copies his files byte for byte; a test and `flash.py --glove` refuse a stale copy; the weights header is placed in both locations, so his current include and the corrected one both work. Only the `-glove` environments link the module, so the owner's boards without sensors keep testing the chain with the dummy module (gated or not).

The source of each decision (the entry numbers in the archived design record) is in the design-reason part of each chapter in `docs/handoff-details/`.

---

Observed on 2026-10-16, before the deploy: with the laptop on Wi-Fi without the NUS VPN, `stujump.comp.nus.edu.sg:22` was reachable but offered only public-key login (`Authentications that can continue: publickey` with `ssh -v`), so the deploy tool failed twice at the first hop with `FAILED: BadAuthenticationType: Bad authentication type; allowed types: ['publickey']`, and nothing was uploaded or changed. With the VPN connected, stujump offered password login and both logins worked (9.2). The board's login shell also printed `Could not chdir to home directory /home/xilinx: Permission denied` and `bash: /home/xilinx/.bashrc: Permission denied`; this comes from the board's home-folder permissions, is harmless, and the tool works under `BOARD_ROOT`.

Version 3 is a new protocol, so all ends must move to it together (only the newest version is supported). **Any step below that logs in to the jump host or the Ultra96 can be done only by the owner, in their own terminal** (see section 9).

Windows kept its bond; no re-pairing | Nothing before the two-board run (step 5) | (done) |

The 28 September version-2 service (PID 105159, session `week7-demo`) was stopped. Steps:

1. **Board service and certificate in one step. Done on 2026-10-16 at 13:06 +08:00 (`status: deployed`; 6.1).** Repeat it for a redeploy (Wen Ree's model, the next renewed certificate, or a restart before V1). From the repository root on the laptop, with the NUS VPN on and `main` checked out (`comms/protocol-v3` is no longer used for work; 6.2). The tool needs `paramiko` for both SSH hops and the upload; `requirements-dev.txt` lists it (`paramiko>=2.8,<4`, added with the documents after `3b94990`), and on this laptop it comes with Anaconda, version 2.8.1. The commands (the second is the deploy run on 2026-10-16):

   ```powershell
   python -m tools.deploy_service --dry-run
   python -m tools.deploy_service --server-cert-dir "$HOME\.codex\private\cg4002-comms-server-20261014"
   ```

   The first command only prints the plan. The second asks for the jump-host and board passwords (typed by the owner; nothing is stored), uploads `common/` and `ultra96/` into a new folder `BOARD_ROOT/source-co-v3-<UTC stamp>`, and then, on the board: compiles it; checks that the new certificate and key match and do not expire within an hour; checks that the running `ultra96.server` is this user's, under `BOARD_ROOT`, and owns exactly `127.0.0.1:8888` and `:9999` (or that both ports are free); copies its code aside; nothing has changed on the board up to here. It then stops the old process with SIGTERM and waits until it has exited (after 10 s, SIGKILL once it has checked the PID is still the same process); backs up the old certificate pair to `BOARD_ROOT/tls/backup-<stamp>/` and installs the new one (key mode 600); starts the new service in the background with `--session-id ltc-comms` and an event log; waits until it listens on both ports. Any failure from the stop onwards stops the new service, restores the old certificate pair and restarts the previous code from the rollback copy. From the last check of the running process just before the stop (`tools/deploy_service.py:244` onwards), the final record (`deployed`, `rolled_back`, `failed_old_still_running`, `failed_nothing_running` or `rollback_failed_nothing_running`) is always printed and saved on the board in `BOARD_ROOT/evidence/active-deployment.json`. A refusal in the checks before it (more than one service of this user, a service outside `BOARD_ROOT`, ports not owned exactly or busy, a compile failure, a certificate and key that do not match or expire within an hour) leaves the running service and the certificate untouched (only the uploaded folder stays), prints the error and exits nonzero, and leaves no record on the board or under `.comms-local/deployments/`. If the laptop hears nothing for 2 × start timeout + 120 s, it says so and asks you to read that file (`python demo.py service`) before trying again. The board's record is printed and saved under `.comms-local/deployments/`, which `demo.py service --start` then follows. Without `--server-cert-dir`, the certificate on the board is kept, and the run refuses to deploy if that certificate is expired. Later, `--model MODULE --model-path DIR` starts Wen Ree's model instead of the dummy one. Then `python demo.py service` shows the new process, its folder and the two loopback listeners (optional; not run on 2026-10-16, because the deploy output already showed the process). The board-side half was tested on Linux (6.1), and the first run against the real board was on 2026-10-16. If the tool fails at once with `FAILED: BadAuthenticationType: Bad authentication type; allowed types: ['publickey']`, the NUS VPN is off: nothing has been uploaded or changed, and since 2026-10-16 the tool also prints a hint to connect the VPN (9.2). The board's login shell prints `Could not chdir to home directory /home/xilinx: Permission denied` and `bash: /home/xilinx/.bashrc: Permission denied`; both are harmless (6.1).
2. **Board firmware. Right board: all three checks below done on 2026-10-16, the plain and the `-glove` check again after the `gloveBegin()` retry was added (D38), and the board left with the plain build (6.1). Left board: the plain build flashed and `python -m tools.check_board left` passed on 2026-10-17 at about 01:40 +08:00, through its existing bond, and the board left with that build (6.1); the gated and `-glove` checks were not run there (optional, because the same firmware passed them on the right board).**
   - **Plain build.** Plug in one board alone and confirm its factory MAC (left `38:18:2b:19:82:ac`, Bluetooth `38:18:2B:19:82:AE`; right `38:18:2b:18:9d:68`, Bluetooth `38:18:2B:18:9D:6A`). Then `platformio run -d firmware/esp32 -e firebeetle32-right -t upload --upload-port COMx` (or `-left`) and `python -m tools.check_board right` (or `left`). Expected: the serial lines `glove_begin ok=1 module=dummy gated=0 attempts=1` and `comms_ready … glove=dummy gated=0`, and `CHECK PASSED … gated=no idle=0 unread=0 refused=0` (right board, 2026-10-16: `CHECK PASSED`, 3022 = 3022 = 3022 before the retry; with it, `attempts=1` and 3033 = 3033 = 3033; left board, 2026-10-17: `attempts=1` and 3025 = 3025 = 3025). Or run the full `python flash.py` with both boards (left first). If Windows asks for an authenticated bond after the firmware change: type `erase-bonds` on the board's serial port, remove the board in Windows Bluetooth settings, then run `python flash.py --pair left` (or `right`).
   - **Gated dummy check (one board).** In PowerShell: `$env:PLATFORMIO_BUILD_FLAGS = "-DCOMMS_DUMMY_MOVING_MS=3000 -DCOMMS_DUMMY_IDLE_MS=7000"`, then the same upload command, then `python -m tools.check_board right`. Expected: `glove_begin ok=1 module=dummy gated=1 attempts=1`; samples for 3 s, then 7 s of silence, repeating; `CHECK PASSED` with `gated=yes` and `idle` above 0, generated = received = acknowledged, and every command answered (right board, 2026-10-16, before the retry, so its boot line had no `attempts` field: as expected; 900 = 900 = 900, `idle` 2140, longest silence 7.03 s, 3/3 commands). Afterwards `Remove-Item Env:PLATFORMIO_BUILD_FLAGS` and flash the plain build again. A two-board `python demo.py run` with gated boards should also pass by the gated coverage rule (4.4).
   - **A `-glove` build on a board without sensors (one board).** `python -m tools.sync_glove_module --check`, then `platformio run -d firmware/esp32 -e firebeetle32-right-glove -t upload --upload-port COMx`. Expected: `glove_begin_failed attempt=1 retry_ms=500`, `glove_begin_failed attempt=2 retry_ms=500`, then `glove_begin ok=0 module=external gated=1 attempts=3` about 1.7 s after reset (three fast tries, no 5 s calibration wait; D38), and the board still advertises and pairs. It sends no samples: every tick counts as `unread`, so `tools.check_board` fails with "nothing acknowledged" (`--duration 20 --commands 2` keeps it short). Right board, 2026-10-16, before the retry: `glove_begin ok=0` 0.71 s after reset (one try), 50 Hz confirmed, 2/2 commands, 753 ticks all `unread`; with the retry: as expected, `attempts=3` at 1.71 s, 2/2 commands, 765 ticks all `unread`. Then flash the plain build again.

Done on the morning of 2026-10-16 on the owner's Mac (Xcode 26.2, Swift 6.2.3), with no build error and no source change; the settings-save fix of that afternoon (4.6) changed the source and was tested again on macOS.** `swift test --package-path ios-visualizer/CommsNative`, with Xcode's Swift 6.1 or newer. 4. **Phone app.** Needs a Mac and Xcode. On the owner's Mac `python` is not on PATH; use `python3` (the system Python 3.9.6 runs both scripts). **Done on 2026-10-16: an unsigned build first; then, in the afternoon, a signed build installed and launched on the owner's iPhone, and, after the settings-save fix, the phone's first subscription to the Ultra96:**
   - `python3 ios-visualizer/tools/patch_export.py` (`EXPORT_PATCH_OK: already applied`) and `python3 ios-visualizer/tools/configure_xcode.py` (`Configured local CommsNative package; signing stays operator-owned.`) ran on the Unity export and changed nothing. Both scripts are fork only and can be rerun; the existing `ios-visualizer/xcode-export/` is already patched and configured (the local package `../CommsNative`, its product `CommsNative` linked into `UnityFramework`, an empty development team).
   - The export's `Package.resolved` held the tested pins but a stale origin hash, so Xcode floated two dependencies beyond them; the origin hash was replaced, and Xcode now keeps the tested pins (9.2; 6.5, D32; in `24dbc1c`, 6.2).
   - From the repository root, `xcodebuild -project ios-visualizer/xcode-export/Unity-iPhone.xcodeproj -scheme Unity-iPhone -destination 'generic/platform=iOS' CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO build` succeeded. The scheme builds `ReleaseForRunning` for devices only (`SUPPORTED_PLATFORMS = iphoneos`, arm64), so it cannot be built for the simulator. The app is `unityTutorial.app` under `~/Library/Developer/Xcode/DerivedData/Unity-iPhone-*/Build/Products/ReleaseForRunning-iphoneos/` (display name `Week 7 Visualizer`, bundle identifier `com.CookingCompany.unityTutorial`, iOS 15.0 or later); the signed builds below replaced the unsigned one there. It links the `Comms*` functions, `CommsNextResult` included, but nothing in this export calls `CommsNextResult` (4.6).
   - The build rewrote three tracked IL2CPP files; they were restored (9.2).
   - **Signed build (13:13 +08:00, an agent with the owner present).** The owner's iPhone, "Wang Yanjie's iPhone" (iPhone 14 Pro, `iPhone15,2`, iOS 26.6.1), was connected by USB with Developer Mode on. The same export with the current `CommsNative` was built signed for it with `xcodebuild -project ios-visualizer/xcode-export/Unity-iPhone.xcodeproj -scheme Unity-iPhone -destination 'id=<the iPhone's UDID>' -allowProvisioningUpdates DEVELOPMENT_TEAM=B25YW25PNS build`, using the owner's free Personal Team and the Apple Development certificate already in Xcode on the Mac; no Apple ID or password was handled by the agent. The team is given on the command line, so nothing is written into `project.pbxproj`. That provisioning profile was valid until 2026-10-23 05:13:10 UTC (13:13 +08:00); a free Personal Team profile lasts 7 days. It was renewed on 2026-10-18 ("To reinstall" below); the renewed profile expires on 2026-10-25 05:25:57 UTC and covers the session of 10-20, after which the app must be rebuilt and reinstalled (9.2).
   - **Installed and launched.** `xcrun devicectl device install app` installed it over the Week 7 build of 2026-09-28 (same bundle identifier, so an upgrade: the app's data was kept, including the saved user names and the CA under `Application Support/Week7/`, which the new build reads, 4.6). The first launch was refused until the owner trusted the developer certificate on the phone (Settings > General > VPN & Device Management; 9.2); then `xcrun devicectl device process launch` ran it.

The app with the fix was rebuilt with the same signed command (signed 14:44:35, same profile and expiry) and reinstalled, and the three IL2CPP files were restored again (9.2).

No samples were sent then; the first results reached the phone in the acceptance run of 2026-10-17 (step 5).

   **To reinstall** (before the profile expires on 2026-10-25 05:25:57 UTC, after any change to the Swift sources, or on another iPhone): connect the iPhone by USB with Developer Mode on and run the signed build and the `devicectl` install in 11 with the team of whoever installs (the owner's is `B25YW25PNS`), whose Xcode must hold that team's Apple Development certificate. Afterwards restore the three IL2CPP files (9.2). To renew before the profile expires, see 9.2: Xcode keeps reusing a cached profile that is still valid. **Renewed on 2026-10-18** (13:25 +08:00, by an agent at the owner's request, with the owner's team): built from `029f079`; the first build reused the cached profile of 10-16, so that profile was moved aside (not deleted) and the second build got a new one, expiring 2026-10-25 05:25:57 UTC (13:25 +08:00); installed with `devicectl` over the existing app (same bundle identifier, the app's data kept) and launched, with no errors in its console. After the reinstall the owner tapped Connect and typed the passwords (setup saved at 13:34:16 +08:00, seen with `devicectl device info files`), and the phone showed `Subscribed` at about 13:36 (read by the owner; the app logs neither its status nor `Received`, so the agent could not see either). A two-board run followed (10 Hz, 60 s, from the laptop by another agent session, as relayed to this one): `CAPTURE PASSED`; the left board 602 samples generated, received and acknowledged, the right board 605; no loss and no disconnects; `Phone expected increase` 1207, and the owner read `Received` 0 before and 1207 after, an exact match (evidence on the laptop in `.comms-local/B07-20261018T053818071684Z-33343989/`, git-ignored). The phone app stayed running in the foreground throughout (no resign or crash in its console). This is the owner's test app; Jie Ling's game build follows 7.3.
5. **Acceptance. Passed on 2026-10-17 (about 01:57 and 01:59 +08:00; 6.1), with dummy data and the built-in dummy model:** the owner opened `python demo.py tunnel` with the NUS VPN on and typed the passwords, the phone reached `Subscribed`, and `python demo.py run` gave `CAPTURE PASSED` at 10 Hz: left 602 = 602 = 602, right 602 = 602 = 602, `Phone expected increase` 1204, phone `Received` 0 to 1204, and `python demo.py run --rate 50` gave `CAPTURE PASSED` (left 3032 = 3032 = 3032, right 3003 = 3003 = 3003, `Phone expected increase` 6035, phone `Received` 1204 to 7239), exact matches both times. Repeat it after any redeploy, reflash or app reinstall, and with Wen Ree's model (then compare with the board's `result_write_complete` count, below). `python demo.py tunnel` (terminal A); on the phone, with the NUS VPN on and the app in the foreground, tap Connect, wait for `Subscribed` and note `Received`; `python demo.py run` (terminal B), then again with `--rate 50`, the gloves' rate. **Pass criterion: the data is delivered, nothing more (the owner's decision, 2026-10-16; 6.5, D39).** Glove → board: the run must print `CAPTURE PASSED` (generated = received = acknowledged for each board, no loss). Board → phone: every result the model emits on the board must reach the phone unchanged. With the built-in dummy model, which the board runs now, that means the increase in the phone's `Received` equals the `Phone expected increase` in the output. With a real model, the increase must equal the board's `result_write_complete` count for the run, and every `model_result` must have been written (no drop event); no tool tallies these from the board yet (planned as an option of `python demo.py service`, before the Monday 10-19 dry run; 13). How many results the model produces, their labels and confidence, and whether the game acts on them, are not Communications' criterion. Run no rehearsal or probe against the board at the same time (9.2): `tools.rehearse_remote_comms` subscribes to the gateway itself and would replace the phone's subscription (one subscriber at a time), so it cannot feed the phone.
6. **Siqiang's gloves (with Siqiang).** `python flash.py --glove`: it checks the module copies, builds both `-glove` environments, and flashes the left glove first, then the right. It writes the gloves' Bluetooth addresses into `.comms-local/boards.json`, replacing those of the owner's boards (9.2). Pair with `python flash.py --pair`. Hold each glove still at power-up: it calibrates for about 5 s before it starts advertising. If a calibration read fails, the board prints `glove_begin_failed attempt=<n> retry_ms=500` and calibrates again (up to three tries; D38), so a glove that fails twice advertises up to about 10 to 11 s later than normal; keep it still until it advertises (on the serial port, `glove_begin ok=1 … attempts=<n>`). `glove_begin ok=0 … attempts=3` means every try failed (no IMU answering, a WHO_AM_I other than `0x70` (7.1, point 5), or a fault on every try), and the glove will send nothing. Then `python -m tools.check_board left` and `right` while moving the glove (an idle glove sends nothing), and a two-glove capture at 50 Hz: `python demo.py run --rate 50` (without `--rate`, `demo.py run` sets both gloves to its default 10 Hz). For a recording session that keeps IDLE samples too, set `$env:PLATFORMIO_BUILD_FLAGS = "-DCOMMS_GLOVE_SEND_ALL=1"` before `python flash.py --glove`, capture with `python demo.py run --rate 50`, export with `python -m tools.export_capture`, then remove the variable and flash the gated build again.

To check whether the board's certificate has expired (with the tunnel open):

```powershell
openssl s_client -connect 127.0.0.1:18889 -servername ultra96.week7.internal
```

### 6.4 Integration progress and schedule

The team meets in person on **Tue 2026-10-20** to integrate V1 together. The schedule below works back from that day. All teammate dates are the owner's proposals until the teammates confirm them in their replies. Teammates deliver code by pushing it to the team repo on GitHub, not over Telegram. ### 6.5 Decisions taken on 2026-10-14 (evening), 2026-10-15 and 2026-10-16, with reasons and status

The owner asked for all work that does not depend on teammates' replies, with technical decisions made within Communications and integration, recorded with reasons. Status words: **verified** = run or measured; **built** = compiled and tested on the laptop, not on the Ultra96 or the phone; **written** = not compiled (no toolchain); **provisional** = waits for a teammate's answer. Since 2026-10-15 the Swift client is compiled and tested on Linux (Swift 6.1 container, 72 tests), and since 2026-10-16 on macOS (73 tests; 76 with the settings-save fix) and for iOS (the iOS Simulator, and the Unity export on the owner's Mac); that day it was also installed on the owner's iPhone in the rebuilt Week 7 app, which subscribed to the Ultra96, and its 34 transport tests passed on that iPhone (in the preview's test host app); on 2026-10-17 it received the results of the acceptance run (6.3 step 5); the rows say so where it matters. Since 2026-10-16 the version-3 service runs on the Ultra96 and the right board runs the current firmware, so several rows now say what ran there; "built" in a row still means that part has not run on hardware or on the Ultra96.
