| D36 | Resolve local `main` by resetting the redundant local merge `33e9e1d` onto `origin/main` (`79ac1f4`) | The fork's `main` had already merged the same team main and also had `24dbc1c` and `b82669b`; the local merge differed only by lacking them, so the reset keeps one history with the fork and loses nothing (6.2) | Verified (10-16): local `main` equalled `origin/main` (`79ac1f4`) before the afternoon's work was committed on it as `8923a59` |
| D37 | The phone client saves its public settings and CA into the old `Application Support/Week7` folder, under the Week 7 names, when creating `Application Support/Comms` fails with no write permission (`CocoaError.fileWriteNoPermission`) and `Week7` exists and is writable (`writableTarget()` in `Configuration.swift`); any other error is still reported, and the form's save message now ends with the underlying error (never the passwords) | On the owner's iPhone the upgraded app's container has a root-owned `Application Support` (mode 0755) whose `Week7` folder stays writable, so the first Connect could not save and could not connect; `load()` already reads the Week 7 files while the `Comms` ones are missing, so saving there keeps saving and reading in agreement without touching anything outside the app's own folder | Verified: 3 new tests, 76 macOS tests (10-16); on the owner's iPhone the Connect after the fix saved and reached `Subscribed` (10-16); not re-run on Linux or the iOS Simulator; in `87feca3` on the fork's `main`, and in the team main since pull request #20 (`be97a04`; 76 macOS tests on its branch) |
| D38 | The firmware calls `gloveBegin()` up to 3 times at boot, pausing 500 ms between tries, before giving up: build switches `COMMS_GLOVE_BEGIN_ATTEMPTS` (default 3, 1 to 10) and `COMMS_GLOVE_BEGIN_RETRY_MS` (default 500, 0 to 10000), the template `beginWithRetries` in `comms_glove.h`, called in `setup()` (`firmware/esp32/src/main.cpp:441-448`). Each failed try that another follows prints `glove_begin_failed attempt=<n> retry_ms=<ms>`; the boot line gains `attempts=<n>`. Decided by Claude at the owner's request (2026-10-16: "u make the decision and record") | In Siqiang's module one failed IMU read during calibration, or a failed WHO_AM_I or register read, makes `gloveBegin()` return false (7.1, point 3). With one call, a single I2C glitch at power-up left the glove sending nothing until the next power-up. How Communications calls his API is Communications' side; the root cause stays in his module (FYI for Siqiang, his decision). A second call is safe: his `gloveBegin()` resets its own state first (samples, quaternion, biases, flex), and `Wire.begin()` on a bus already started returns true in the Arduino-ESP32 core 2.0.17 used here. Cost: each retry repeats his calibration (about 5 s, glove held still), so a glove that fails twice during calibration advertises up to about 10 to 11 s later than normal; with no sensor the three fast tries add about 1 s (the two pauses). It does not fix a WHO_AM_I other than `0x70` (7.1, point 5), which fails every try, or a fault present on every try | Built (host test `testBeginRetries`; `tests/test_firmware_glove.py` 13 passed; full suite 848 passed, 4 skipped); verified on the right board (10-16: the sensorless `-glove` build made three tries, `glove_begin ok=0 … attempts=3` at 1.71 s, and still advertised, connected through its existing bond and answered commands; the plain build printed `attempts=1` and passed `tools.check_board`, 3033 = 3033 = 3033). Not verified: a real glove whose first try fails and a later try succeeds (the host test covers that path) |
| D39 | Communications passes when the data is delivered, nothing more (the owner's decision, 2026-10-16, in the owner's words: "on my side is, just the data can be delivered"). (a) Glove → board: every sample a glove sends is received by the laptop and acknowledged by the board, generated = received = acknowledged per glove, no loss (`CAPTURE PASSED` of `demo.py run`, `CHECK PASSED` of `tools.check_board`). (b) Board → phone: every result the model emits on the board reaches the phone unchanged. With the built-in dummy model, (b) is checked as before: the phone's `Received` increase equals `Phone expected increase` (one result per accepted sample or command). With a real model, (b) = the phone's `Received` increase equals the board's `result_write_complete` count for that run, and every `model_result` was written (no drop event such as `result_no_subscriber`, `result_drop_oldest`, `result_drop_stale` or `result_send_failed`) | Communications' job is to send each module's data out and make sure the next module receives it accurately (3). How many results the model produces, their labels, confidence or accuracy, and whether the game acts on them, are not Communications' criterion: they belong to the model's and the game's owners. With a real model the laptop cannot know how many results to expect, but the board's event log already records each result the model emitted (`model_result`), each result written to the phone (`result_write_complete`) and each drop (`ultra96/diagnostics.py`, the `_observe` calls in `ultra96/server.py`) | The owner's decision. Checked with the dummy model on 10-17: the acceptance run passed (a) and (b) at 10 Hz and 50 Hz (6.3 step 5). Open Communications task: a tool that reads and tallies these events from the board after a run does not exist yet (planned as an option of `python demo.py service`), due before the Monday 10-19 dry run. The exact sample-to-result match of the rehearsal tools still holds only with the dummy model |

What these decisions do **not** settle (section 13): the scales, the out-of-range policy, the model's function names and in-process use, the label set and result rate, whether the MOVING onset lag suits the model, the `CommsNextResult` limits, and which WHO_AM_I value each glove reports (7.1, point 5). The IDLE/MOVING field is settled: it does not travel (D20). The `gloveBegin()` retry is decided (D38), and so is Communications' pass criterion (D39); the tool that tallies delivered results for real-model runs is still to be written (D39).

---

## 7. The three interfaces to connect

Communications specifies only which functions and fields are needed at each hand-over point. Function names and internal implementation are the other side's decision. Who does what is summarised in the table in 3.1.

**What exists (2026-10-16; the code is in local `main`, the fork and, through pull requests #19 (`7d8317a`) and #20 (`be97a04`, the phone client's settings-save fix of that afternoon), the team main (6.2), except the `gloveBegin()` retry of D38, which is on the fork's `main` (pushed from the laptop on 2026-10-16) but not in the team main; the board service is deployed on the Ultra96, and the rebuilt phone app is installed on the owner's iPhone and subscribed to it on the afternoon of 2026-10-16):** the Communications side of 7.1 and 7.2 is built: the gating, the sample policy and the scaling are host-tested with the dummy module, and on 2026-10-16 the gating ran on the right board with the gated dummy module (6.1). The `-glove` firmware with Siqiang's module compiles and links; on 2026-10-16 it ran on the owner's right board, which has no sensors, so only the path where `gloveBegin()` fails ran (`glove_begin ok=0`, since the retry of D38 after three tries; the board still advertised, connected through its existing bond and answered commands). It has not run on a glove. The model interface on the board (`start(emit)`, `on_sample`, the thread-safe `emit`, `--model`) is built and tested on the laptop, and since 2026-10-16 it runs on the Ultra96 with the built-in dummy model. Every receiver accepts model labels and real confidence. The Communications side of 7.3, `CommsNextResult`, is compiled and tested on Linux and macOS, built for iOS (2026-10-16: the iOS Simulator, and the Unity export on the owner's Mac) and installed on the owner's iPhone in the rebuilt Week 7 app, which does not call it. **On the teammates' side, only Siqiang's sensor module exists** (team commit `51593be`); there is no model module and no game script calling the bridge. The Ultra96 runs the version-3 service since 2026-10-16 (session `ltc-comms`, built-in dummy model; 6.3). Since 2026-10-16 the owner's iPhone runs the rebuilt app (6.3 step 4), which subscribed to the redeployed service; a phone that still has the Week 7 build has only the three C functions, under their Week 7 names (`Week7Start`, `Week7CopyDisplay`, `Week7Stop`), accepts only `REST`, `FIST`, `OPEN`, `POINT` with confidence 1.0, and its session `week7-demo` cannot subscribe to the redeployed service. The messages to the teammates were written before this work and describe these parts as planned. Since 2026-10-16 the board side of 7.2 runs on the Ultra96 with the dummy model, but no teammate component is connected to any of these parts yet.

The interface shapes and dates below are the owner's proposals of 2026-10-14. They become commitments when the teammates confirm them in their replies; Siqiang's part (7.1) is delivered. Teammates deliver code by pushing it to the team repo on GitHub, not by sending files over Telegram.

### 7.1 From Siqiang: the glove sensor module (called by the Communications firmware)

**Delivered on 2026-10-15** (team commit `51593be`, `hardware/gloves/integration_files/`). It is built into the `-glove` firmware but has not yet run on a glove. On 2026-10-16 the right `-glove` build ran on the owner's right board, which has no sensors: `gloveBegin()` returned false (`glove_begin ok=0` 0.71 s after reset; after the retry of D38 was added, three tries and `glove_begin ok=0 module=external gated=1 attempts=3` at 1.71 s), and the board still advertised, connected through its existing bond and answered commands, and sent nothing (6.1). Division of work (the owner's direction of 2026-10-14, matching what Siqiang delivered): Siqiang delivers the sensor module (initialisation, reading one sample, the movement decision). **olsonwangyj** calls it from the Communications firmware and is responsible for scaling to int16, packing, BLE sending and connection management. The capture algorithm, calibration, the movement detector and sensor problems stay with Siqiang.

| Function | Delivered (`glove_sensor.h`) | How Communications calls it | Contract (from his header and code) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Initialise | `bool gloveBegin()` | First in `setup()`, before BLE starts; while it returns `false`, called again, up to 3 tries 500 ms apart (since 2026-10-16, D38) | I2C (400 kHz) and ADC set-up, then gyro calibration: a 2 s settle and 1000 readings, about 5.1 s in all (answer B), during which the glove must be still. Returns `false` promptly when the IMU does not answer, so the board still advertises after the last try (seen on the owner's board without sensors, 2026-10-16). Also returns `false` when the IMU's WHO_AM_I is not exactly `0x70` (point 5 below), or when one calibration read fails (point 3). It resets its own state first, and `Wire.begin()` on a bus already started returns true, so calling it again after `false` is safe (D38) |
| Periodic update | `void gloveUpdate()` | Once per main-loop pass, roughly every 1 to 2 ms, connected or not, outside the connection mutex | Reads the IMU at most once per millisecond (2 ms I2C timeout) and updates the Mahony filter, the flex values (every 20 ms) and the movement classifier (one sample every 20 ms). No `delay()` |
| Read one sample | `bool gloveRead(float out[11])` | At each send tick, about every 20 ms at 50 Hz, **only while the laptop is connected and subscribed**, so there can be seconds between calls | `true`: `out[]` holds the latest valid sample. `false` (with `out[]` zeroed): no valid sample, or the latest is older than 100 ms |
| Movement decision | `bool gloveIsMoving()`; also `bool gloveMovementReady()` | As `gloveShouldSend()` (`firmware/esp32/src/glove_module.cpp`), right after a successful `gloveRead()`; `gloveMovementReady()` is not called | `true` = MOVING: the classifier is ready and its smoothed state is MOVING. False until the first 1 s window has filled |

Apart from the calibration in `gloveBegin()`, none of the functions waits; a call takes at most about 10 to 20 ms, usually far less (answer C).

Order and units of the 11 values: `ax, ay, az` (g, gravity removed), `gx, gy, gz` (°/s, bias removed), `yaw, pitch, roll` (degrees), `flex1, flex2` (0.0 to 1.0). With the scale factors in 4.2, each value must stay within: acceleration ±3.2767 g, angular rate ±327.67 °/s, angles ±327.67°, flex at most 3.2767. With the current settings in the module (the same as in `imu_v7.ino`: ±2 g, so about ±3 g after gravity removal, a margin of only about 0.28 g; ±250 °/s; yaw and roll ±180°, pitch ±90°; flex 0 to 1), normal values are expected to fit, but that is not guaranteed: the final value depends on the module's processing (for example the gyro bias subtraction). How an out-of-range value is handled is to be agreed with Wen Ree (8, step 1).

**How Communications calls it (built 2026-10-15; on 2026-10-16 it ran on the owner's right board without sensors, where `gloveBegin()` failed as expected; not yet run on a glove):** the `-glove` environments build an unchanged copy of the module (`firmware/esp32/lib/glove_sensor/`, made by `python -m tools.sync_glove_module`; 4.3). `setup()` calls `gloveBegin()` first, up to three times until it returns `true` (D38); `loop()` calls `gloveUpdate()` every pass; at each send tick `produceSample()` calls `gloveRead()`, then `gloveShouldSend()` (his `gloveIsMoving()`), then `prepareSample` (which scales with `scaleSample`), then `serializePacket`. A `false` from `gloveRead`, an IDLE decision or a refused sample sends nothing and takes no `seq`. With `-DCOMMS_GLOVE_SEND_ALL=1` every valid sample is sent, IDLE included, for recording sessions. His three sample functions have exactly the signatures Communications proposed, so only the movement decision needed the one-line adapter.

Constraints the Communications firmware places on the module (from the existing code). The delivered module meets them:

- PlatformIO, Arduino framework, board `firebeetle32`, Arduino-ESP32 core 2.0.17 (PlatformIO espressif32 7.1.0).
- The module does not define `setup()`/`loop()`. It does not call `Serial.begin()` and does not read the serial port (the serial port is used for pairing). It does not use `BluetoothSerial`, BLE or Wi-Fi (the radio belongs to the Communications firmware). It does not print to serial for each sample.
- `seq`, `uptime_ms` and the left/right identity are filled in by the Communications firmware. The module does not deal with them (answer D: his 12th value and Bluetooth names were only for his PC).

**Issues found in the module (2026-10-15 and 2026-10-16; reported to the owner; Siqiang's files are untouched).** None blocks the Communications build. Points 1, 2, 3 and 5 are about his module; point 4 is a property for Wen Ree. Whether and how to raise them with Siqiang is the owner's decision (messages to teammates stay on interfaces and dates, section 10).

1. **The include path does not resolve where the file lives.** `hardware/gloves/integration_files/glove_sensor.cpp:4` is `#include "../../../movement_model_params.h"`. From `hardware/gloves/integration_files/` that path points at the repository root, where no such file exists, so the file does not compile in its own folder. The header is in the same folder, so `#include "movement_model_params.h"` would fix it. Communications builds copies in the layout the current path expects (the header at `firmware/esp32/movement_model_params.h`), and the sync tool also puts the header beside the copied `.cpp`, so the corrected include works too.
2. **Any failed reading restarts the movement classifier.** In `gloveUpdate()`, more than 100 ms between calls (`glove_sensor.cpp:487-489`), a failed IMU read (`:494-496`) or a non-finite sample (`:533-535`) calls `resetMovementClassifier()` (`:301-311`). That empties the 1 s window and sets the state to not ready and not MOVING. `gloveIsMoving()` then stays false until a new window has filled and two evaluations agree, at least about 1.2 s (a 1 s window, then a second evaluation 200 ms later). For that long the glove sends nothing, even in the middle of a gesture. In the Communications firmware `gloveUpdate()` runs every 1 to 2 ms, so only two things trigger it: the first call after boot (BLE set-up runs between `gloveBegin()` and the first `gloveUpdate()`; harmless, as the window is still empty) and transient I2C failures (2 ms I2C timeout). A possible change, for Siqiang to decide: skip a single failed or non-finite sample instead of resetting, and reset only after a longer outage.
3. **One failed read during calibration makes `gloveBegin()` fail (mitigated in Communications by the retry, D38; the root cause is still his, FYI).** `calibrateGyroscope()` returns false as soon as any one of its 1000 IMU reads fails (`glove_sensor.cpp:171`), and `gloveBegin()` then returns false (`:464`); a failed WHO_AM_I or register read does the same. Until 2026-10-16 the Communications firmware called `gloveBegin()` once at boot, so after such a glitch the glove counted every tick as unread and sent nothing until it was power-cycled. Since 2026-10-16 `setup()` calls it up to three times, 500 ms apart (`firmware/esp32/src/main.cpp:441-448`, `beginWithRetries` in `comms_glove.h`; decided by Claude at the owner's request, 6.5 D38). Each retry repeats his calibration (about 5 s, glove held still), so a glove that fails twice advertises up to about 10 to 11 s later than normal; a missing IMU still fails promptly, and the three tries then add about 1 s. A fault present on every try still leaves the glove without data. Skipping a single failed calibration read inside his module remains a possible fix on his side, his decision.
4. **A design property, for Wen Ree (7.2):** MOVING needs two consecutive above-threshold evaluations 200 ms apart, so it comes at best about 0.2 to 0.4 s after motion starts, later for gentle motion; IDLE needs three consecutive below-threshold evaluations, so a still tail of at least about 0.4 s, possibly 1 s or more, is sent after each movement (`glove_sensor.cpp:381-389`, `:412-416`). The exact lags need real data. Samples before the MOVING decision are never sent. The two gloves gate independently.
5. **`gloveBegin()` accepts only WHO_AM_I `0x70` (FYI for Siqiang; his component, interface effect only).** `gloveBegin()` returns false unless the IMU's WHO_AM_I register reads exactly `0x70` (`EXPECTED_WHO_AM_I`, `glove_sensor.cpp:25`; the check at `:447-449`), the MPU-6500 value. His authoritative sketch (answer A, `hardware/gloves/model_threshold_variant_1/debug_esp32_ml_movement_detector_v1.ino:608-610`) only prints a warning for another value and carries on. Effect in Communications: a glove whose IMU reports another WHO_AM_I (for example a compatible MPU variant) starts with `glove_begin ok=0` (`attempts=3` with the current firmware), counts every tick as unread and sends nothing, like the owner's board without sensors on 2026-10-16 (6.1). The retry of D38 does not help here: the check fails on every try, so the three tries only add about 1 s. The code cannot tell which value the gloves' IMUs report. Question for Siqiang: which WHO_AM_I value each of the two gloves prints in his sketch.

**Still asked of Siqiang:** real samples for the scale check (a log from his own sketch, or a recording through the chain: a build with `-DCOMMS_GLOVE_SEND_ALL=1`, `python demo.py run --rate 50`, then `python -m tools.export_capture`); both gloves for flashing with `python flash.py --glove` and a joint test (Sat 10-17 or Sun 10-18, Mon morning at the latest), left with the owner until Tuesday if possible (6.3 step 6); which WHO_AM_I value each glove prints (point 5); and, if the owner raises them, whether he changes points 1, 2, 3 and 5. Once the Communications firmware is on a glove, his Bluetooth Classic recording script can no longer connect to it. His answers to the earlier questions (authoritative sketch, timing, call times, left/right, IDLE/MOVING, recording) are in 2.3.

### 7.2 From Wen Ree: the model's entry and exit on the board (called by the Communications service on the board)

The connection point is `ultra96/model.py` and the call in `ultra96/server.py` (line 329). **The Communications side is built (2026-10-14) and, since 2026-10-16, runs on the Ultra96 with the built-in dummy model; Wen Ree's module does not exist yet.** The service loads it with `--model MODULE` (and `--model-path DIR` for where it lives on the board), checks that `start` and `on_sample` are plain callables (not `async def`), and without `--model` uses its built-in dummy model. Suggested shape (names are theirs to choose; if Wen Ree picks other names, only `ultra96/model.py` changes):

| Function | Provided by | Called by whom, when | Contract |
|---|---|---|---|
| `start(emit)` | Wen Ree | Called once by the Communications service at start-up, before the first sample | Load the model here, and tell Communications how long that takes. `emit` is the result exit that Communications hands to the model |
| `on_sample(device_id, boot_id, seq, uptime_ms, values)` | Wen Ree | Called by Communications once for each newly accepted sample, on the service's single thread. The return value is unused | **Must return immediately.** The service is a single asyncio event loop, and the ACK is written only after this returns. If it is slow, the ACKs of both gloves are held up (the laptop waits at most 5 s for an ACK; sample freshness is 2 s). Inference must run on another thread or in another process |
| `emit(gesture, confidence, device_id, boot_id, seq)` | **Communications** (built) | Called by the model once per result. It may be called from any thread | Communications moves the call safely back onto the event loop (the result queue has no lock). Returns `True` when the arguments are valid and the service is running, `False` otherwise; numpy-style numbers are accepted. Refused results are counted on the board (`model_results_rejected`, `model_results_duplicate`, `model_results_late`). Invalid, unknown-identity and duplicate results also log a warning, at most one per kind per second; results emitted after the service stopped are only counted |

`on_sample` fields:

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `device_id` | 1 or 2 | 1 = left, 2 = right |
| `boot_id` | uint32 | A random number for this power-up of this glove. It changes when the glove restarts |
| `seq` | uint32 | Sample sequence number within this power-up |
| `uptime_ms` | uint32 | This glove's own millisecond clock. The two gloves are not synchronised |
| `values` | 11 floats | Order and units as in 7.1. The owner's direction (2026-10-14), to be confirmed by Wen Ree: physical floats. The values travel as int16; Communications decodes them and divides by the scale, so the model gets the agreed physical units. Restoring cannot recover precision dropped when packing: acceleration and flex keep 4 decimal places, angular rate and angles 2. Any normalisation the model needs is Wen Ree's |

What Communications guarantees: within one power-up (same `device_id` + `boot_id`), `seq` only increases and never repeats. After a glove restart, `boot_id` changes and `seq` starts again from 0, so track by `(device_id, boot_id)`. Samples can be missing, and the missing ones show as gaps in `seq`: lost over the radio; evicted when the laptop's 64-place queue for that glove is full; dropped after waiting more than 2 s on the laptop (not resent); or lost when a link drops (on a BLE disconnect the laptop discards that glove's queued samples, and on a TLS reconnect the samples already written but not acknowledged count as ambiguous drops and are not resent; that includes the first frame sent after the board closed a silent ingest connection, after 600 s or earlier when it needs the slot, 4.5 and 9.2). While a glove is disconnected it produces no samples and `seq` does not advance, so the disconnected time itself shows only as a jump in `uptime_ms`. The two gloves arrive over two independent connections, **with no pairing and no alignment**. Pairing, building windows and deciding gesture or idle all happen after the entry point, on the model side.

**What reaches the model since gating (2026-10-15; built; on 2026-10-16 the gating ran on the right board with the gated dummy module, but Siqiang's detector has not run on a glove):**

- **Only samples the glove's detector calls MOVING.** While a hand is still, its glove sends nothing, so `on_sample` gets nothing from it. A pause shows as a jump in `uptime_ms`; `seq` has no gap there, because a skipped tick takes no number. A `seq` gap still means a lost sample.
- **The two gloves decide independently.** One glove can send while the other is silent, so a 50-step window may hold samples from one hand only.
- **The start of a movement is not sent.** From Siqiang's code (`glove_sensor.cpp:381-389`, `:412-416`), MOVING needs two consecutive above-threshold evaluations 200 ms apart, so at best it comes about 0.2 to 0.4 s after a movement starts, and later for a gentle movement. IDLE needs three consecutive below-threshold evaluations, so it comes at least about 0.4 to 0.6 s after the 1 s window stops scoring as motion, and that window still holds the movement for up to 1 s after it stops. So a still tail of at least about 0.4 s, possibly 1 s or more, is sent after each movement. The exact lags need real data. Samples before the MOVING decision are never sent, and the link cannot send them later (about 66 samples/s per glove is the measured ceiling, so there is no room for a backlog). After a failed sensor reading, a glove may send nothing for about 1.2 s or more (7.1, point 2).
- In the `-glove` builds each glove starts at 50 Hz. `python demo.py run` without `--rate` sets both to 10 Hz (9.2).

Questions for Wen Ree about this (drafted for the owner on 2026-10-15; whether they were sent is for the owner to record in 6.4): whether the onset lag is acceptable for the model's windows, or whether the model needs the start of each movement (then it is a change on the detector side, to agree with Siqiang); and how the model treats a window in which one glove is silent.

`emit` fields:

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `gesture` | string | The label text as it is (the field name on the wire is `gesture`): 1 to 32 characters from `A–Z a–z 0–9 _ -` |
| `confidence` | number from 0 to 1 | Confidence, passed on unrounded |
| `device_id`, `boot_id`, `seq` | as above | The identity of **one** sample this result used (usually the newest one in the window; either glove is fine). Communications uses it to build `result_id` (`device_id:boot_id:seq`). **Each result must point to a different sample**: the phone de-duplicates by `result_id`, and a second result with the same identity is dropped |

The result message cannot gain fields freely. The field set of `GESTURE_RESULT` is matched exactly in four places (the board, the iPhone and the two reference receivers), so the only correlation information is one sample's identity. Communications forwards the label and the confidence as they are, without filtering or renaming. Results, like samples, are not resent. The board drops a result when no phone is subscribed at the moment it is produced. When 32 results are already waiting, the oldest is dropped (`ResultQueue.put`). It also drops a queued result that has waited 2 s or more on the board (`get_timed`). The write to the phone's connection gets only what is left of those 2 s (`_send_results`). If the write does not finish in what is left of those 2 s, the board does more than drop that result: it ends that phone's subscription (`result_send_failed`, `reason=error`), abandons what is still queued, and the phone reconnects and subscribes again. This limits waiting on the board only. It is **not** a promise about when, or whether, the phone receives the result: bytes already handed to TCP/TLS cannot be recalled, and the phone keeps its own 2 s display window counted from when it received the result. There is no end-to-end latency bound (9.2).

What Communications checks on this hand-over is delivery only (the owner's decision, 6.5 D39): every sample a glove sends is acknowledged by the board (the ACK of a stream sample is written only after `on_sample` returned, 4.7), and every result the board accepts from `emit` (event `model_result`) reaches the phone unchanged. How many results the model produces, their labels, confidence and accuracy are not Communications' criterion. With a real model this is judged on the board's event log (the phone's `Received` increase against the `result_write_complete` count; 6.3 step 5); the tool that tallies it is still to be written (8, step 6).

Whether the model can run directly inside the Communications service process: the service runs as the ordinary user `xilinx` (no sudo) with `/usr/bin/python3` (3.10), and uses only the standard library itself. If the model code runs in this process, everything it imports (numpy, pynq and so on) must load under this interpreter and this user. `fpga/README_FPGA_PYNQ.md` says the overlay is loaded with sudo and the pynq-venv Python. If the model can only run that way, it is a separate process, and Communications defines a format for passing the same fields between the two processes on the board. That is the owner's work, not a new network route.

Asked of Wen Ree in the message (once the owner sends it): whether it can run inside the Communications process (module and function names) or must be a separate process, and which packages it depends on; the full label set (by default the six in `models/class_names.json`); how often a result is produced (`src/live_inference.py` currently, once 50 samples have accumulated, produces one result for every new sample, including those with confidence below 0.70, and has no "idle" label; is the board version the same?); a first version that can be connected by Fri 10-16 (even one that always returns the same label), pushed to the team repo: for the in-process option, the module and function names; for the separate-process option, how to start it, its dependencies, and its input and output in the format the owner sends on Thu 10-15; and how out-of-range values should be handled (8, step 1).

### 7.3 For Jie Ling: the interface Communications provides (called by the game)

Proposed (the owner's direction, 2026-10-14; final when Jie Ling confirms): **Jie Ling does everything in Unity and the iOS build, on their own Mac and iPhone: the C# that calls the Communications functions, the game logic, exporting the project, adding `CommsNative` to the Xcode project, building, and installing the app. olsonwangyj provides `CommsNative` in the team repo (`ios-visualizer/CommsNative`), pushes the new parts there, and sends short steps for adding it to the Xcode project.** Patching an export is no longer used, and no export is passed between people. Communications **needs no API from the game**. The game calls the three existing Communications C functions (section 4.6), plus the fourth one, `CommsNextResult` (in the source since 2026-10-14; compiled and tested on Linux since 2026-10-15; tested on macOS, built for iOS and installed on the owner's iPhone on 2026-10-16, in the rebuilt Week 7 app, which does not call it), and can use the Week 7 receiver as an example. On the phone, Jie Ling uses the NUS VPN, logs in in the app with their own jump-host and board accounts, and imports the CA file the owner shares (9.2). The VPN must be on before Connect: off the VPN the jump host offers no password login, and the app then ends the session instead of retrying (from the code; not yet seen on a phone; 9.2). With the VPN on, the owner's iPhone subscribed on 2026-10-16 (6.1). The CA did not change with the certificate renewal, so a CA already imported stays valid. A first install creates the app's `Application Support` folder itself; an app installed over an existing container can meet a root-owned one, as on the owner's iPhone, and the client then saves into the old `Week7` folder if that exists and is writable, and otherwise shows the error (4.6, 9.2). The message asks Jie Ling to have it installed before the integration session on Tue 10-20 and to tell the owner about any problem at any time; the only question in it is whether the timing works.

C# declarations of the three functions (derived from the C signatures; not yet compiled in Unity). These symbols exist only in the iOS build. Calling them in the Unity Editor or on other platforms throws an exception, so calls go inside `#if UNITY_IOS && !UNITY_EDITOR`:

```csharp
[DllImport("__Internal")] static extern void CommsStart();
[DllImport("__Internal")] static extern int  CommsCopyDisplay(byte[] buffer, int capacity);
[DllImport("__Internal")] static extern void CommsStop();
```

- `CommsStart`: call once at start-up. It places the `Communications Connect` button in the top-right corner of the screen and does not connect by itself. A person taps the button, enters the jump-host and board login details, and imports the CA. Keep that corner clear.
- `CommsCopyDisplay`: call once per frame on the main thread with a 2048-byte buffer. It returns the number of UTF-8 bytes written (not counting the terminating 0). When nothing changed, or the buffer is null or too small, it returns 0 and writes nothing, and the game should keep the text it already has.
- `CommsStop`: call only when the app closes. It ends the session, clears the passwords and removes the button. Do not call it on a scene change or when going to the background.
- Line 2 of the text is the status. `Subscribed` means only that the subscription handshake with the Ultra96 succeeded; it does not prove that results are flowing.
- Line 3 holds only the latest result and is overwritten by the next one, so results that arrive between two polls can be lost (4.6). Use it to watch results while testing, not as game input. Line 1 is `Communications` in the current source, which the app installed on the owner's iPhone on 2026-10-16 was built from (the Week 7 build of 2026-09-28 showed `Week 7 • DUMMY inference` and `confidence 1.0`); line 3 shows the received confidence (`DisplayMailbox.swift:16-18`). Do not parse the display text. Ids starting with `cmd:` come only from the owner's command tests on the laptop.
- **Per-result delivery is Communications' job; `CommsNextResult` is in the source (2026-10-14), compiled and tested on Linux (2026-10-15), tested on macOS, built for iOS and installed on the owner's iPhone in the rebuilt Week 7 app (2026-10-16), which does not call it.** The game's action entry points are one-shot: in `vis/Assets/Scripts/Core/GestureButtonController.cs` the `On...Pressed` methods are wired to the on-screen buttons' `OnClick()`, and its comment says Ultra96 gestures will call them later. So the game needs results one at a time, not a display that the next result overwrites. The owner's direction (2026-10-14): add a fourth bridge function that hands out structured results one at a time. Proposed shape:

  ```c
  int32_t CommsNextResult(char* buffer, int32_t capacity);
  ```

  It removes the oldest result still waiting and writes it as one compact UTF-8 JSON object with a terminating 0, for example `{"result_id":"1:305419896:42","gesture":"CHOP","confidence":0.93,"device_id":1}`, and returns the number of bytes written (not counting the 0). It returns 0 when no result is waiting. If the buffer is null or too small for the next result (it needs the bytes plus the terminating 0), it writes nothing, leaves the result in the queue and returns minus (bytes + 1), the capacity needed; with a 512-byte buffer this does not happen with the expected labels. The game calls it in a loop each frame while it returns a positive number. In C#: `[DllImport("__Internal")] static extern int CommsNextResult(byte[] buffer, int capacity);`. It exists in `DisplayMailbox.swift` since 2026-10-14 and was compiled and tested on Linux on 2026-10-15 (4.6); on 2026-10-16 it was tested on macOS and built for iOS on the owner's Mac, and the `UnityFramework` of the rebuilt Week 7 export, installed on the owner's iPhone the same day, defines it, though nothing in that export calls it (4.6; 6.3 steps 3 and 4). `CommsNative` with it is in the team repo's main branch since 2026-10-16 (pull request #19, merge commit `7d8317a`; with the settings-save fix of that afternoon (4.6) since pull request #20, merge commit `be97a04`; 6.2), ahead of the plan of Sun 10-18 (6.4); the short steps for adding it to the export are not written yet (8 step 7). Jie Ling can write the C# against the declaration now.

  What it promises, and what it does not: results wait in a queue in arrival order, de-duplicated by `result_id`, and each result that is not dropped can be taken out at most once. It does not promise that every result is delivered: results lost before the phone (board drops, reconnects, no subscriber) never arrive, and the queue itself drops results, counting each drop. Provisional V1 limits, the same rules the board uses: at most 32 results wait; when full, the oldest is dropped; a result still waiting 2 s after it reached the phone is dropped. At 50 results a second, 32 places last only about 0.64 s if the game stops reading, so the limits are revisited once Wen Ree says how often results come. The queue is emptied when the session stops (Disconnect, the app becoming inactive, `CommsStop`) and when a new Connect starts; an automatic reconnect keeps it, and its entries still expire after 2 s (also after a terminal error, which goes through the same path as a reconnect status). A null buffer counts as too small. Expired and dropped results are counted (`pendingExpired`, `pendingDropped` in the display snapshot; both reset on a new Connect, like `Received`). Expiry also happens while only `CommsCopyDisplay` is polled, so results that nobody takes out are dropped after 2 s rather than kept. Whether several identical labels in a row mean one action or several is for Wen Ree and Jie Ling to agree; Communications hands out every result as it is.
- Only one object may call `CommsCopyDisplay` (each call reports only the change since the previous call), and that object must survive scene changes. The same object would call `CommsNextResult`.
- What to do with a result (which action it maps to, cooldowns, repeats) is the game's business.
- The phone can no longer rely on the 5005 listener. Results come in only through the Communications bridge functions: the three that exist today and, once a game build that calls it is installed, `CommsNextResult`, which is the game input.
- Passwords are not saved. As soon as the app stops being active (going to the background, screen lock, pulling down Control Center or Notification Center, an incoming call), it disconnects and clears the passwords, and they must be entered again. Nothing in the Communications client or in the game's current scripts keeps the screen awake (neither `idleTimerDisabled` nor Unity's `Screen.sleepTimeout` is set anywhere in the repository), so set Auto-Lock to Never on the phone in use.
- Reference: in Week 7 the owner had no Unity project and patched the generated C++ (`TlsDataReceiver`) of one Xcode export. Jie Ling's own C# was never changed. The patched export is only on the fork. The patch script <https://github.com/olsonwangyj/Let-Them-Cook/blob/main/ios-visualizer/tools/patch_export.py> is for reference only and cannot be run against a new project.

Until a game build that calls `CommsNextResult` is installed (the function is built for iOS since 2026-10-16; the `UnityFramework` of the rebuilt Week 7 export defines it, but nothing in that export calls it; that export has been installed on the owner's iPhone since 2026-10-16), the game can show the text and test the connection, but it cannot drive actions reliably from it.

The IDLE/MOVING question is answered without a packet change: a glove sends only while MOVING, and the state does not travel (D20). **Still open:** the scales are checked against Siqiang's real samples before the layout is frozen. In normal use every value is expected to fit, but that is not guaranteed: the final value depends on the module's processing (for example the gyro calibration bias is subtracted), not only on the raw sensor range. **Out-of-range values are to be agreed with Wen Ree**: the firmware supports both answers (clamp, the default, or drop with `-DCOMMS_OUT_OF_RANGE_DROP=1`), counts what it clamps or refuses, and never drops a connection or a whole gesture. A clamped value cannot be told apart from a real value at the limit (for example 3.2767 g can be either); if the model needs to know which samples were clamped, a per-sample flag is needed, which again changes the packet and `on_sample`. Neither option guarantees that a gesture is still recognised. **Only the newest version is supported (the owner's rule, 2026-10-14):** an upgrade means reflashing both boards, redeploying the Ultra96 service and reinstalling the phone app together; an old phone build rejects version-3 results.

## 9. Prohibitions and cautions

### 9.1 Things not to do

1. **Do not change teammates' code.** `hardware/`, `src/`, `models/`, `data/`, `reports/`, `fpga/`, `vis/`, `export_fpga_reference.py` and `requirements.txt` are read-only. That includes changes that look harmless: line endings, formatting, dependency versions, regenerated files. When integration really needs a teammate to change code, write down exactly what to change and why, and give it to olsonwangyj to raise with them.
2. **Do not change how Communications connects, and do not propose alternatives or fallback routes.** BLE to the laptop, the laptop and the phone each connecting to the Ultra96 over SSH, the phone client handing results to the game through three C functions: these are settled, and they are designed for the NUS firewall rules. In particular, do not propose "the laptop connects to the phone's port 5005", "a phone hotspot", "a direct connection on a shared LAN" or the like. Other modules adapt to Communications.
3. **Do not overstep.** Do not do or supply any part of a teammate's implementation in the owner's name (for example, writing inference code for the model owner or C# scripts for the game owner). Do not list pairing, windowing, gesture decisions or label mapping as the owner's tasks. Do not judge the quality of teammates' components on the owner's behalf.
4. **Do not touch passwords.** Do not enter, store (not in files, memory or commit messages) or repeat the jump-host or Ultra96 passwords, even if the owner pastes them into the conversation and says they may be used. For any step that logs in, give the command and let the owner run it in their own terminal.
5. **Do not push to the team repository (`upstream`)** unless the owner asks for it explicitly. Pushing to the fork (`origin`) is fine.
6. **Do not delete files the owner has not put under version control.** To clean up, move them to `D:\LetThemCook-attic\`. `.week7-local/` holds historical evidence and copies of the firmware flashed in September; keep it.
7. **Do not run `pytest` without paths from the repository root.** It collects `fpga/hls/export_conv1d_test.py`, which rewrites a tracked file as soon as it is imported. Use `python -m pytest tests laptop/tests phone/tests -q -p no:cacheprovider`.
8. **Do not touch the `week7` names in the Week 7 receiver** (see 4.6 and the table in 6.2).
9. **Do not run a second subscriber during a demo or a joint test** (`laptop/phone_simulator.py`, `tools/rehearse_remote_comms.py`). The board has only one subscriber slot, and they would push the phone off.
10. **Refer to teammates by name or "they". Do not guess anyone's gender from their name.**

### 9.2 Pitfalls

- **The certificate lasts only 30 days.** The renewed one, on the board since 2026-10-16, expires on **2026-11-13 14:27:35 UTC**, before the final demo around Week 13, so renew again in early November (`--renew-server-from`, then `tools.deploy_service --server-cert-dir`). After it expires the service still starts, but the laptop gets no ACK at all and the phone stops at `SSH, TLS or protocol verification failed`. `tools.deploy_service` refuses to deploy with a certificate that expires within an hour. Running the certificate tool without `--renew-server-from` replaces the CA too, and then the fingerprint in the phone source and the laptop's `--ca` must both change.
- **The session ID must match on all three ends.** On a mismatch the board closes the connection. The symptom is `received` rising while `acked` stays at 0, or the phone bouncing between `Connecting` and `Disconnected`.
- **Port defaults differ.** `demo.py` uses 18889. Running modules such as `laptop.dual_bridge` or `tools.ssh_tunnel` directly defaults to 18888. The tunnel and the capture must use the same port.
- **Check that the tunnel is listening before `python demo.py run`.** On 2026-10-17 a first acceptance attempt ran with no tunnel on `127.0.0.1:18889`: both boards connected and streamed, but nothing was acknowledged (`transport_connections` 0, `transport_errors` 11) and the run ended `CAPTURE NOT PASSED` (6.1). Keep the `python demo.py tunnel` terminal open and confirm it is up (for example `Test-NetConnection 127.0.0.1 -Port 18889`) before starting the capture.
- **Personal defaults are written in the code.** The default account in `demo.py tunnel`, the default path of `demo.py --ca`, the account and board paths in `tools/demo_service.py` (these cannot be changed with arguments), the jump-host account `yanjie` and `BOARD_ROOT` used by `tools/deploy_service.py` (`--jump-user`, `--board-user` change the accounts), and the two boards' Bluetooth addresses in `tools/demo_boards.py`. Watch these when the person, the machine or the boards change.
- **`demo.py service --start` follows the newest local deployment record** (`.comms-local/deployments/`, written by `tools.deploy_service`; the first one, `deployment-20261016T050629Z.json`, was written on 2026-10-16) and falls back to `BOARD_SOURCE`, the 28 September snapshot. A deployment made by hand, or from another laptop, leaves no record here, so `--start` would then start the old code.
- **The service process does not reclaim identity capacity.** Each glove power-up takes one `(device_id, boot_id)`; so does each synthetic rehearsal and each probe that sends samples. A process allows at most 128; when they run out, the service must be restarted. A restart clears the de-duplication state. The service deployed on 2026-10-16 had used 5 by the end of that day; restart it (redeploy, 6.3 step 1) before V1.
- **With no subscriber, results are dropped at once.** So get the phone to `Subscribed` first, then start the capture.
- **Run one rehearsal or probe against the board at a time (found 2026-10-16).** The gateway sends every result, whatever input caused it, to its one subscriber. Any other input during a rehearsal (another rehearsal, a probe, `demo.py run`, a board left streaming) adds results the rehearsal did not cause, and its exact correlation fails: the first BLE rehearsal through the Ultra96 saw the two results of an idle test running at the same time (`1:2725017464:1` and `:2`) as unexpected (6.1). A second subscriber also replaces the first (9.1, item 9): `tools.rehearse_remote_comms` subscribes to the gateway itself, so it pushes a subscribed phone off and cannot feed the phone; the acceptance run with the phone uses `demo.py run` (6.3 step 5).
- **Off the NUS VPN, the jump host offers only public-key login, so password logins fail (found 2026-10-16).** Without the VPN, `stujump.comp.nus.edu.sg:22` is still reachable, but it offers only `publickey` (`Authentications that can continue: publickey` with `ssh -v`); with the VPN on, it offers `password`. So a missing VPN does not show as an unreachable host but as a refused login method. The laptop has no SSH key pair, and nothing in Communications uses keys: the route stays password logins with the VPN on. What each part does:
  - `tools.deploy_service` fails at the first hop with `FAILED: BadAuthenticationType: Bad authentication type; allowed types: ['publickey']`; nothing is uploaded or changed. Since 2026-10-16 it also prints "The server does not offer password login. Off the NUS VPN, stujump accepts only public keys: connect the NUS VPN and run this again." (exit code 2; 6.5, D33).
  - The phone (from the code; not observed on a phone): `Trust.swift:27` fails with "does not offer password authentication" (`SSHRoute.swift:49`), and `CommsClient.swift:189` treats a password failure as terminal: no automatic retry, the passwords are cleared, and the user must connect the VPN and log in again. So the phone's own VPN must be on before Connect (with it on, the owner's iPhone logged in through both hops and subscribed on 2026-10-16). If the VPN drops during a session while stujump stays reachable, the next automatic reconnect meets the same refusal and ends in this terminal state, instead of retrying until the VPN is back.
- **A Week 7 build of the iPhone app cannot subscribe to the redeployed service (since 2026-10-16).** It uses session `week7-demo`, and the service now uses `ltc-comms`, so the board closes its connection (the session pitfall above). On the owner's iPhone the rebuilt app replaced it on 2026-10-16 (6.3 step 4); a phone that still has the Week 7 build needs the rebuilt app.
- **The board's login shell prints two permission errors** (`Could not chdir to home directory /home/xilinx: Permission denied` and `bash: /home/xilinx/.bashrc: Permission denied`; seen 2026-10-16). They come from the board's home-folder permissions, are harmless, and the tools work under `BOARD_ROOT`.
- **Silent ingest connections stay open for up to 600 s (since 2026-10-15).** A laptop that vanished (sleep, a VPN drop) can leave its connection open on the board for up to 600 s, because sshd may keep the socket; such half-open connections take some of the 8 client slots. When all 8 are taken, the board closes the ingest connection that has waited longest (at least 5 s) for a new client (`idle_evicted`, with a warning log line). A live glove that is only still and is closed this way (or after 600 s) reconnects when it next sends. The laptop reads ACKs only while frames are pending (`laptop/bridge.py:599-624`), so it notices the close only then: the first frame written after the close (usually only that one) counts as an ambiguous drop. The samples that arrive during the back-off of 0.5 s or more (`:668-673`) and the TLS reconnect wait in the 64-place queue (the oldest is dropped when it is full) and are dropped if older than 2 s. The event also raises `transport_errors` and `ambiguous_dropped` (and `ack_errors` when the ACK read fails), so that capture is not clean.
- **After the GATT table changes or the firmware is reflashed, Windows may need to pair again.** Recovery is manual: `erase-bonds` on the board's serial port → remove the board in Windows → pair again.
- **At high rates the actual packet rate is below the setting** (the 70 Hz setting gives about 66 packets/s). From 75 Hz up, packets are lost.
- **Whoever holds the phone logs in with their own account.** Any teammate can log the phone in with their own NUS VPN, jump-host and board accounts; it does not have to be olsonwangyj's phone or account (the owner's correction, 2026-10-14). Each person types only their own passwords; nobody shares a password. Because the passwords are not saved, the person holding the phone must be able to log in again whenever the app stops being active. Whoever builds the app installs it from their own Mac with their own Apple ID; with a free Apple ID the installed app expires after 7 days and is installed again.
- **The phone works only in the foreground.** Locking the screen or switching away disconnects it and clears the passwords. Nothing keeps the screen awake, so set Auto-Lock to Never on the phone in use (7.3).
- **There is no end-to-end timestamp.** The 2 s freshness is each segment's own waiting time, not end-to-end latency.
- **The model's `on_sample` runs on the service's only event loop, before the ACK.** A model that does its inference inside `on_sample` holds up the ACKs of both gloves (the laptop waits at most 5 s; sample freshness is 2 s). The counters `model_slow_calls` and `model_max_call_ms` in the `stopped` line show it; inference belongs on another thread or in another process. An `async def on_sample` is refused when the model loads. A model whose threads are not daemon threads no longer keeps a stopped service alive (`exit_with_model_threads`), but its threads end abruptly then.
- **The board service writes one INFO log line for each accepted input.** With both gloves at 50 Hz that is about 100 lines per second. They go to the service's output: the foreground SSH terminal when it was started with `demo.py service --start`, or the log file under `BOARD_ROOT/evidence/` when it was started in the background by `co_deploy.py` or `tools.deploy_service` (`server-co-<stamp>.log`, or `rollback-server-<stamp>.log` after a rollback). A service started by `tools.deploy_service` also writes an event log (`observer-co-live-<stamp>`) unless `--no-event-log` is given.
- **`flash.py` rewrites the Git-tracked `comms_fixtures.h` every time.** After changing `common/dummy_fixtures.json` you must reflash before the data on the board changes.
- **A leftover `LTC_COMMS_SESSION` in the terminal.** Documents from the transition period taught setting `LTC_COMMS_SESSION=week7-demo`. This variable affects `demo.py run`, `live` and `service --start` together, but not the phone. If it is still set, the laptop and a newly started service both use `week7-demo`. `CAPTURE PASSED` still appears, but the phone stays at `Disconnected: connection closed; retrying`. When troubleshooting, first make sure this variable is not set.
- **Neither end records why something was rejected.** For a bad frame, a session mismatch, an identity conflict or exhausted capacity, the board only closes the connection and adds 1 to `rejected`; it writes no log entry, and it prints its metrics only once, when the service stops. On the laptop, an ACK timeout or a mismatched ACK increments both `ack_errors` and `transport_errors`; a TLS connect or handshake failure increments only `transport_errors`. Neither counter records a reason. So an expired certificate and an unreachable port look the same (only `transport_errors`), and a session mismatch and a protocol version mismatch between the two ends look the same (the board closes the connection after the frame, which counts as both). For the 38-byte migration, both ends must be upgraded together.
- **The phone's "automatic retry" logs in to both SSH hops again and again.** When the board service is not running, the session does not match, or another subscriber has pushed the phone off, the phone treats it as a closed connection. It then logs in to the jump host and the board again with the passwords, after a delay that starts at 0.5 s and doubles up to 5 s (reset after a result arrives), with no limit on attempts. When the service is not ready, tap Disconnect first; do not leave the app on retrying.
- **A service started with `demo.py service --start` is the foreground process of that SSH session.** Closing that terminal, the laptop going to sleep or the VPN dropping all make the service exit. Keep the terminal that started the service open, like the tunnel terminal. This applies only to the `--start` path. The service on the board since 2026-10-16 was started in the background by `tools.deploy_service` and does not depend on any SSH session; so did the 2026-09-28 process started by `.week7-local/co_deploy.py`, which that deploy stopped (4.5).
- **A different laptop needs `known_hosts` first.** Both SSH hops use strict host-key checking, and nothing in the repository writes `known_hosts`. The two public keys in the phone source `Configuration.swift` can be used to check them.
- **The single-board diagnostic tools cannot choose a board.** `laptop.ble_counter_receiver`, `laptop.mtu_probe` and `tools.rehearse_comms --ble` scan by service UUID and connect to whichever board they find first. The two boards advertise the same name, so power only one board when using these tools. They also do not set the rate, so the board keeps its last rate. `tools.check_board left|right` (2026-10-14) connects by the board's address and sets the rate, so the other board may stay powered.
- **Symptoms when a board still has old firmware.** The two-board capture reads the source statistics first. A value that is not 36 bytes long (version-2 firmware's `LCS1`, and the 24-byte `LCS3` of the 2026-10-14 build) is reported as `source statistics must contain exactly 36 bytes; flash this board with the current firmware`; a 36-byte value with another marker as `invalid source statistics magic; flash this board with the current firmware`. Week 7 firmware (`W7S1`) is refused too. That board is not subscribed, and start-up times out after 30 s. Neither board is in this state since 2026-10-17: both run the current firmware (6.3).
- **Glove builds use 87.6% of the flash** (1148437 bytes; the dummy build 85.6%). About 160 KB are left for the module and the firmware to grow; watch the PlatformIO size line after any change.
- **A `-glove` build on a board without sensors sends nothing.** `gloveBegin()` reports no IMU on each of its three tries (two `glove_begin_failed attempt=<n> retry_ms=500` lines, then `glove_begin ok=0 module=external gated=1 attempts=3`, about 1.7 s after reset; D38), every send tick counts as `unread`, and `tools.check_board` fails with "nothing acknowledged" (seen on the right board on 2026-10-16: 753 ticks, all `unread`, with one try; 765 with the retry). The same happens on a glove whose IMU reports a WHO_AM_I other than `0x70`, or whose calibration fails on all three tries (7.1, points 3 and 5); a single failed calibration read is retried. Use the plain environments on the owner's boards, and the `-glove` ones only on Siqiang's gloves.
- **A gated glove is silent while the player is still, and the first part of each movement is not sent** (7.2). A board in calibration does not advertise yet: hold the glove still for about 5 s after power-up. If a calibration read fails, the firmware calibrates again (up to three tries, 500 ms apart; D38), so a glove that fails twice advertises up to about 10 to 11 s later than normal; the serial port shows `glove_begin_failed attempt=<n> retry_ms=500` for each repeat, and the boot line's `attempts=<n>` says how many tries it took. After a failed sensor reading the glove may send nothing for about 1.2 s or more (7.1, point 2).
- **`python demo.py run` sets both boards to 10 Hz unless `--rate` is given.** The `-glove` builds start at 50 Hz, the model's rate; run `python demo.py run --rate 50` with the gloves. The rate a capture sets stays until the board reboots.
- **`python flash.py --glove` rewrites `.comms-local/boards.json` with the gloves' addresses**, like any full `flash.py` run. Captures and `tools.check_board` then address the gloves, not the owner's boards; run `python flash.py` again with the owner's boards to switch back.
- **Stale copies of Siqiang's module.** The firmware builds copies, never the files in `hardware/`. After Siqiang changes his module in the team repo, merge the team main and run `python -m tools.sync_glove_module`. Until then `tests/test_glove_module_sync.py` fails and `flash.py --glove` refuses to build. Never edit the copies.
- **A timing-sensitive test.** `tests/test_server_diagnostics.py::test_close_deadline_includes_status_persistence` (a 0.01 s close, a 0.2 s wait) failed once under load on 2026-10-15 and passed on the rerun. Rerun it alone before treating it as a regression.
- **Captures made before version 3 cannot be re-checked with the current code** (`demo.py report`, `tools/video_evidence.py`); use `c42b386` or `5a004c6` (the team main after PR #18) for them.
- **The opt-in Linux deploy test (`B07_LINUX_DEPLOY_TEST=1`) starts and stops `ultra96.server` processes inside the local WSL Ubuntu.** Never run `tests/linux/deploy_remote_test.sh` on the Ultra96 itself.
- **For agents: escapes can be rewritten on the way into a file.** On 2026-10-14 the tool layer turned `\u` escapes and `\n` inside strings passed through shell here-documents into literal characters in three cases. After writing test vectors or strings with backslash escapes, compare the file's bytes with what was meant (`git diff`, `py_compile`).
- **`verify-import.py` only works on a freshly imported, unpatched Unity export.** Running it on the already-patched `xcode-export/` reports `IMPORT_CHECK_FAILED`; this is expected.
- **Every build of the Unity export rewrites three tracked IL2CPP files** (found 2026-10-16) with machine-specific content: `ios-visualizer/xcode-export/Il2CppOutputProject/Source/il2cppOutput/compile-data.json` (output path, job count and SDK path, for example `iPhoneOS26.5.sdk` on the exporter's Mac against `iPhoneOS26.2.sdk` on the owner's Mac), and `il2cpp-compile.traceevents` and `profile.json` in the same folder. They are not part of any change: after every build, before committing, restore them with `git checkout --` and the three paths. (`Il2CppTempDirArtifacts/` is ignored by Git.)
- **A stale `Package.resolved` in the export lets Xcode float the Swift dependencies** (found and fixed 2026-10-16). `ios-visualizer/xcode-export/Unity-iPhone.xcodeproj/project.xcworkspace/xcshareddata/swiftpm/Package.resolved` held the tested pins but still carried the origin hash of the old `Week7Native` package graph, so the first time Xcode opened the project it treated the file as out of date, resolved again and moved two transitive packages beyond the tested pins: swift-asn1 1.7.2 to 1.7.3 and swift-collections 1.6.0 to 1.7.3. The file keeps the same pins as `ios-visualizer/CommsNative/Package.resolved` and now carries the origin hash that Xcode 26.2 computes for the current graph (`61a9e9439e28fdba61954662ce4aebc5969f5aa187c32e2be44de326f34e7160`; the committed change is that one line; 6.5, D32), and `xcodebuild -resolvePackageDependencies` leaves it unchanged. After any change to the package's dependencies, check that the export's file still has the same pins as the package's, so that the app builds against the versions the tests used.
- **Choosing a signing team in Xcode writes it into `project.pbxproj`.** Choosing a team under Signing & Capabilities writes the team ID into `ios-visualizer/xcode-export/Unity-iPhone.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj`, and Xcode 26 may also re-save the whole file (objectVersion 46 to 60 and reformatting, as the change stashed in the Mac clone shows, 6.2). Do not commit either: the committed export keeps the team empty, and signing stays with whoever installs. Rerunning `configure_xcode.py` empties the `DevelopmentTeam` target attributes but leaves any `DEVELOPMENT_TEAM` build setting Xcode wrote, so it does not fully remove a chosen team. Before committing, check the file with `git diff` and restore it with `git checkout --` if it changed. Giving the team on the `xcodebuild` command line instead (`DEVELOPMENT_TEAM=<team ID>` with `-allowProvisioningUpdates`, as on 2026-10-16; commands in 11) writes nothing into the file.
- **A phone app signed with a free Personal Team expires after 7 days.** The builds installed on the owner's iPhone on 2026-10-16 used a profile valid until 2026-10-23 05:13:10 UTC; the rebuild installed on 2026-10-18 uses one that expires on 2026-10-25 05:25:57 UTC (13:25 +08:00). Xcode reuses a cached profile while it is still valid, so a rebuild before expiry keeps the old date: to renew early, move the `com.CookingCompany.unityTutorial` profile out of `~/Library/Developer/Xcode/UserData/Provisioning Profiles/` before the build, and `-allowProvisioningUpdates` then fetches a new 7-day profile. The renewed profile covers the session of 10-20; after that the app must be rebuilt signed and reinstalled (6.3 step 4; commands in 11). Installing over the existing app with the same bundle identifier is an upgrade and keeps the app's data.
- **The first launch needs the developer trusted on the phone, once.** On 2026-10-16 the first launch of the newly signed app was refused until the owner opened Settings > General > VPN & Device Management on the iPhone and trusted the developer certificate; only then did `xcrun devicectl device process launch` start it. This is done by hand on the phone.
- **An upgraded app container can hold a root-owned `Application Support` (found 2026-10-16).** On the owner's iPhone, in the app's container (which dates from 2026-09-21), `Library/Application Support` is owned by root (uid 0, gid 501, mode 0755, dated 2026-09-21) while `Application Support/Week7` inside it is owned by the app and writable, so the app cannot create `Application Support/Comms`; the first Connect then failed with `Could not save the public setup. Try again while the iPhone is unlocked.` (`NSCocoaErrorDomain` 513, `NSPOSIXErrorDomain` 13). Why the folder is owned by root is unknown; nothing done on 2026-10-16 caused it. Since the fix of that day the client saves into the `Week7` folder in that case, and otherwise the message ends with the underlying error (4.6). A first install creates the folder itself, so this concerns only an app installed over an existing container.
- **On-device tests leave a test host app on the phone.** Running the `CommsNativeTransport` tests on the iPhone (2026-10-16) installed their test host app, `Communications Native Preview` (`org.letthemcook.comms.nativepreview`), which stays installed after the run. It is not the game or the Communications receiver; the owner may delete it.
- **On the owner's Mac, `python` is not on PATH.** Use `python3` (`/usr/bin/python3`, Python 3.9.6) for `patch_export.py`, `configure_xcode.py` and `generate_test_pki.py`. The commands written with `python` elsewhere in this document are for this laptop.
- **On the owner's Mac, `/usr/bin/clang++` links against the wrong macOS SDK unless `SDKROOT` is set.** Without it, Xcode 26.2's clang picks the newest Command Line Tools SDK (`/Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/SDKs/MacOSX27.0.sdk`), cannot read its libraries (`unknown architecture arm64e.x1`), and `tests/test_ios_export_patch.py::test_compiled_receiver_hooks_poll_bounded_native_snapshots` fails at the link. With `SDKROOT=$(xcrun --sdk macosx --show-sdk-path)` (Xcode's `MacOSX26.2.sdk`) all 18 tests of that file pass (2026-10-16).

## 10. Misunderstandings the owner has corrected (do not repeat them)

olsonwangyj corrected each of these.

| Wrong | Right |
|---|---|
| Proposing "the laptop connects to the phone's port 5005 over a shared network or a hotspot" | It breaks the NUS firewall rules. The phone connects by itself, through `stujump` over SSH, to the Ultra96 result gateway |
| Proposing alternatives or fallback routes for the connection, or promising to change how the phone client connects | The Communications connection design is the right approach. It does not change and has no fallback |
| Thinking that with the VPN on, the board can be reached directly | With the VPN on, traffic still goes through `stujump`. V1 keeps the jump-host route |
| Treating `phone/unity/CommsPhoneReceiver.cs` as the receiver deployed on the iPhone | It is the Android reference implementation. "Week 7 receiver" means `CommsNative` on the iPhone plus the `Week7*` bridge in the export |
| Proposing to give the NumPy inference code to Wen Ree, or to write C# scripts for Jie Ling | Overstepping. The owner only calls their interfaces; how they implement them is not the owner's concern |
| Writing pairing, windowing, one label per gesture, or label mapping as the owner's work | These are the teammates' work. Communications ends at the interface |
| Saying the link was tested only at 10 Hz, or that the Bluetooth approach is not decided | The 50 Hz and 70 Hz settings were measured clean on 2026-09-28 with the 32-byte dummy packet (70 Hz setting: about 66 packets/s). BLE was chosen long ago |
| Slipping opinions on the quality of teammates' components into messages (model accuracy, gyro range, a path bug, and so on) | Talk only about interfaces and delivery dates |
| Asking teammates questions the code already answers | Read the code first. Ask only what the code cannot answer (physical state, intent, plans) |
| Using a password the owner pasted | Never use, store or repeat it. The owner does the logins |
| Thinking Siqiang writes the firmware after the merge | Siqiang provides the sensor module. The owner writes the merged firmware |
| Wanting to rename the TLS host name, the `Week7*` functions and the like | These are either written into the certificate or part of the Week 7 receiver. Leave them |
| Assuming only the owner can log the phone in, because the jump-host account is personal | Any teammate can log in with their own NUS VPN, jump-host and board accounts; the phone need not be the owner's (2026-10-14) |
| Using gendered pronouns for teammates | Use names or "they" |
