## 11. Running and verifying

**No hardware needed:**

```powershell
python -m pytest tests laptop/tests phone/tests -q -p no:cacheprovider
python -m tools.generate_comms_pki --output-dir <an empty directory outside the repository>      # one-off certificates, for local rehearsal only
python -m tools.rehearse_comms --pki-dir <the same directory> --duration 30 --target 100
python -m tools.sync_glove_module --check                    # the firmware's copies of Siqiang's module are current (exit 0)
python -m tools.export_capture [<capture folder>] [--out <file.csv>] [--device 1|2]   # a capture's samples as CSV
& "$env:USERPROFILE\.dotnet\tools\pwsh.exe" -NoProfile -File phone/tests/run_core_tests.ps1   # C# self-test (PowerShell 7)
```

The rehearsal starts a board service, a bridge and a subscriber on the local machine and prints one JSON line. `passed` should be `true`. With `B07_LINUX_DEPLOY_TEST=1` set, the pytest run also exercises the deploy tool's board-side program in WSL Ubuntu (it starts and stops `ultra96.server` processes there). On 2026-10-15, at `d582ce6`, the suite gave 841 passed, 4 skipped, on 2026-10-16, at `4c6ab4c`, 842 passed, 4 skipped, later that day, on local `main` with the deploy tool's VPN hint, 843 passed, 4 skipped (121.9 s), and with the `gloveBegin()` retry, 848 passed, 4 skipped (all without the opt-in Linux test; with it, the last recorded totals are those of `15de82e`, 780 passed, 3 skipped). The C# self-test prints `PASS 113 C# contract/frame/freshness checks`.

The Swift tests and the iOS builds on a Mac need Xcode with Swift 6.1 or newer, and XcodeGen for the preview app. Run them from the repository root; the comments give the results on the owner's Mac on 2026-10-16 (Xcode 26.2, Swift 6.2.3). Use `python3` there, because `python` is not on PATH, and choose an installed simulator if the device name differs:

```sh
swift test --package-path ios-visualizer/CommsNative                 # macOS build: 76 tests, 0 failures, with the settings-save fix (73 before it; PythonBoardTests included)
python3 ios-visualizer/NativePreview/tools/generate_test_pki.py      # isolated test PKI in the ignored .comms-local/CommsFixturePKI
xcodegen generate --spec ios-visualizer/NativePreview/project.yml   # the ignored CommsNativePreview.xcodeproj
xcodebuild -project ios-visualizer/NativePreview/CommsNativePreview.xcodeproj -scheme CommsNativePreview -destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPhone 17 Pro' -jobs 4 build   # compiles the #if os(iOS) code
xcodebuild -project ios-visualizer/NativePreview/CommsNativePreview.xcodeproj -scheme CommsNativeTransport -destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPhone 17 Pro' -jobs 4 -parallel-testing-enabled NO test   # 34 tests
xcodebuild -project ios-visualizer/NativePreview/CommsNativePreview.xcodeproj -scheme CommsNativePreview -destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPhone 17 Pro' -jobs 4 test   # 1 UI test
xcodebuild -project ios-visualizer/xcode-export/Unity-iPhone.xcodeproj -scheme Unity-iPhone -destination 'generic/platform=iOS' CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO build   # the Unity app, unsigned, for devices only
```

The lines from `generate_test_pki.py` to the UI test are the preview commands of `ios-visualizer/NATIVE-INTEGRATION.md`: the test PKI, `xcodegen`, one build and the two test runs. The last command changes three tracked IL2CPP files; restore them afterwards (9.2).

Signed for an iPhone, installed, launched, and the transport tests on the iPhone (run on 2026-10-16 with the owner's iPhone on USB, Developer Mode on; `<UDID>` is the iPhone's, and `B25YW25PNS` is the owner's free Personal Team, whose Apple Development certificate is in Xcode on the Mac; the test lines need the test PKI and the `xcodegen` project above; 6.3 step 4):

```sh
xcodebuild -project ios-visualizer/xcode-export/Unity-iPhone.xcodeproj -scheme Unity-iPhone -destination 'id=<UDID>' -allowProvisioningUpdates DEVELOPMENT_TEAM=B25YW25PNS build   # signed; nothing written into project.pbxproj
xcrun devicectl device install app --device <UDID> <path to unityTutorial.app>   # the app path in 6.3 step 4; installs over the existing app as an upgrade
xcrun devicectl device process launch --device <UDID> com.CookingCompany.unityTutorial   # refused until the developer is trusted on the phone (9.2)
xcodebuild -project ios-visualizer/NativePreview/CommsNativePreview.xcodeproj -scheme CommsNativeTransport -destination 'id=<UDID>' -allowProvisioningUpdates CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=YES CODE_SIGN_STYLE=Automatic DEVELOPMENT_TEAM=B25YW25PNS build-for-testing
xcodebuild -project ios-visualizer/NativePreview/CommsNativePreview.xcodeproj -scheme CommsNativeTransport -destination 'id=<UDID>' -parallel-testing-enabled NO test-without-building   # 34 tests, 0 failures on iOS 26.6.1
```

The signed Unity build also changes the three IL2CPP files; restore them afterwards (9.2). The on-device tests leave their test host app installed on the phone (9.2). The UI test was not run on the device.

The Swift tests on this laptop run in the Docker image `swift:6.1`, with the package and `ios-visualizer/reference` (two `ConfigurationTests` read a certificate there) mounted read-only and built in a copy inside the container, from the repository root: `docker run --rm -v "${PWD}/ios-visualizer/CommsNative:/src/CommsNative:ro" -v "${PWD}/ios-visualizer/reference:/src/reference:ro" swift:6.1 bash -c "cp -r /src /work && cd /work/CommsNative && swift build --build-tests && swift test"`. That gave 72 tests, 0 failures, on 2026-10-15; it does not cover the iOS-only code (4.6). It was not re-run after the settings-save fix of 2026-10-16; two of its three new tests skip themselves when run as root.

**One board, no Ultra96 (after flashing):**

```powershell
python -m tools.check_board right          # or left; 65 s at 50 Hz, 3 commands; prints CHECK PASSED and gated/idle/unread/refused
```

At each boot the board prints on its serial port (`python flash.py --monitor COMx`) the boot line `glove_begin ok=<0|1> module=<dummy|external> gated=<0|1> attempts=<n>`, then `comms_ready …` (4.3). With the dummy module that is `glove_begin ok=1 module=dummy gated=0 attempts=1` (`gated=1` for the gated dummy); a `-glove` build on a board without sensors first prints `glove_begin_failed attempt=1 retry_ms=500` and `glove_begin_failed attempt=2 retry_ms=500`, then `glove_begin ok=0 module=external gated=1 attempts=3` (D38). The gated dummy build and the `-glove` checks are in 6.3 step 2; the real gloves in 6.3 step 6 (`python flash.py --glove`).

**Deploying the board service (the owner's login, NUS VPN on):** `python -m tools.deploy_service --dry-run`, then `python -m tools.deploy_service [--server-cert-dir <folder>] [--model MODULE --model-path DIR]` (6.3 step 1; run on 2026-10-16 with `--server-cert-dir`). It needs `paramiko` (in `requirements-dev.txt`; on this laptop it comes with Anaconda, version 2.8.1); the dry run does not import it. Off the VPN it fails at the first hop with a hint to connect the VPN (9.2).

**Remote rehearsal through the real Ultra96 (the owner's logins, NUS VPN on; one rehearsal or probe at a time, 9.2).** `tools.rehearse_remote_comms` needs both the ingest and the gateway forward, and `python demo.py tunnel` opens only the ingest one. On 2026-10-16 one OpenSSH process opened both (terminal A, typed by the owner, who entered both passwords; the options are those `tools/ssh_tunnel.py` `tunnel_command` builds, plus the second `-L`; 6.5, D34):

```powershell
ssh -N -T -o StrictHostKeyChecking=yes -o ServerAliveInterval=15 -o ServerAliveCountMax=3 -o BatchMode=no -o Port=22 -o ConnectTimeout=60 -o "ProxyCommand=ssh -o StrictHostKeyChecking=yes -o ServerAliveInterval=15 -o ServerAliveCountMax=3 -o BatchMode=no -o ConnectTimeout=20 -W [%h]:%p yanjie@stujump.comp.nus.edu.sg" -o ExitOnForwardFailure=yes -L 127.0.0.1:18889:127.0.0.1:8888 -L 127.0.0.1:19999:127.0.0.1:9999 xilinx@makerslab-fpga-35.ddns.comp.nus.edu.sg
```

Then, in terminal B, the two rehearsals run on 2026-10-16 (6.1), one after the other. Each takes the result subscription, so the phone must not be connected; the second needs a board on the current firmware, powered, with its Windows bond:

```powershell
python -m tools.rehearse_remote_comms --ca "$HOME/.codex/private/cg4002-week7-20260906/ca-cert.pem" --ingest-port 18889 --gateway-port 19999 --duration 60 --target 0 --confirmed-remote-topology
python -m tools.rehearse_remote_comms --ca "$HOME/.codex/private/cg4002-week7-20260906/ca-cert.pem" --ingest-port 18889 --gateway-port 19999 --duration 60 --target 0 --confirmed-remote-topology --ble --address 38:18:2B:18:9D:6A
```

The first sends synthetic samples; the second relays the right board over BLE (use the left board's address `38:18:2B:19:82:AE` for the left one). Each prints its events as JSON lines and ends with a `summary` line: `passed` and `correlation.exact_match` must both be `true`. The rehearsal does not set the board's rate (about 10 Hz after boot). The idle tests of 2026-10-16 used a scratch script that is not in the repository (6.1).

**Full chain on hardware (connect the VPN first; olsonwangyj does the logins):**

```powershell
python demo.py service          # inspect the board's ports and processes
python demo.py tunnel           # terminal A, keep it open
# iPhone (NUS VPN on, app in the foreground): tap Communications Connect, wait for Subscribed, note Received
python demo.py run              # terminal B
python demo.py report
```

| What to verify | How to check |
|---|---|
| The board received the input (level 3; Communications' criterion, glove → board) | `CAPTURE PASSED`; for each board, Generated = Received = ACKed |
| The phone received the results (level 5; Communications' criterion, board → phone) | With the built-in dummy model: the increase in `Received` on the iPhone equals `Phone expected increase` in the output (compared by a person). With a real model: the increase equals the board's `result_write_complete` count for the run, with every `model_result` written; no tool reads that from the board yet (13) |
| The services bind only to loopback | In the `ss -ltnp` output printed by `demo.py service`, the local address of 8888 and 9999 is `127.0.0.1` |

Communications passes when the first two rows pass: the data is delivered (the owner's decision, 6.5 D39). How many results the model produces, their labels and confidence, and what the game does with them, are judged by the model's and the game's owners.

**Flashing and pairing:** `python flash.py` (left first, then right), `python flash.py --glove` (Siqiang's gloves with his module; left first), `python flash.py --pair left`, `python flash.py --monitor COMx`, `python flash.py --boards`. With the gloves, capture at their rate: `python demo.py run --rate 50`.

Symptoms, causes and recovery for common faults are in `docs/handoff-details/06-tests-operations-extension.md`.

## 12. Code map: what each folder and file does

This section lets a new agent know where things are without re-reading the code. Before changing a file, still read the file itself.

### 12.1 Top level

| Path | Owner | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| `firmware/esp32/` | Communications | BLE peripheral firmware for the two FireBeetle ESP32 boards (PlatformIO project), with the built-in dummy glove module and, in `lib/glove_sensor/`, an unchanged copy of Siqiang's module |
| `common/` | Communications | Python modules shared by the laptop and the Ultra96: packet format, control frames, network frames, TLS |
| `laptop/` | Communications | BLE reception and forwarding (relay) program on the Windows laptop |
| `ultra96/` | Communications | The service on the Ultra96: receives samples, replies with ACKs, pushes results to the phone |
| `phone/` | Communications | Reference and diagnostic receivers (Python, Android C#, iSH scripts). **Not** the one deployed on the iPhone |
| `ios-visualizer/` | Communications | The native iPhone client `CommsNative` (Swift), the patch tools of the Week 7 receiver, a preview app |
| `tools/` | Communications | Operations and rehearsal tools: tunnel, board service, certificates, dummy data, rehearsal, evidence |
| `tests/` | Communications | Communications Python tests (they also compile and run the firmware host tests) |
| `demo.py` | Communications | Day-to-day entry point: `tunnel`, `run`, `live`, `report`, `service` |
| `flash.py` | Communications | Builds and flashes the two boards (`--glove`: Siqiang's gloves with his module), records Bluetooth addresses, pairs, serial monitor |
| `requirements-dev.txt` | Communications | Dependencies for the tests, the certificate tool and the deploy tool (pytest, cryptography, `paramiko>=2.8,<4` for `tools/deploy_service.py`, added with the documents after `3b94990`; the pyserial listed there is not used by Communications code). On this laptop paramiko 2.8.1 comes with Anaconda |
| `docs/` | Communications | olsonwangyj's documents (fork only) |
| `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` | Communications | Rules for agents: ownership table, role boundaries, test command, document rules (fork only) |
| `hardware/` | Siqiang | Glove firmware sketches, recording scripts, schematic, and the sensor module for the Communications firmware (`gloves/integration_files/`). **Read-only** |
| `src/`, `models/`, `data/`, `reports/`, `fpga/`, `export_fpga_reference.py`, `requirements.txt` | Wen Ree | Model training and inference, weights, data, FPGA project. **Read-only** |
| `vis/` | Jie Ling | Unity AR game project. **Read-only** |
| `README.md`, `.gitignore` | Shared | Only Communications lines may be added or changed |

### 12.2 Communications code, file by file

**`common/` (shared by both ends; changing it changes the laptop and the board service at once)**

| File | What it does |
|---|---|
| `sensor.py` | Encoding and decoding of the 38-byte version-3 sensor packet (`SensorPacket`, `encode_packet`, `decode_packet`, `PACKET_MAGIC = b"LC"`, `VERSION = 3`, `CHANNELS`, `SCALES`, `PACKET_SIZE`, `SENSOR_MTU_FLOOR`); `physical_values` (int16 / scale); converting a packet into a `SENSOR_BATCH` message; loading and choosing dummy data |
| `control.py` | Encoding, decoding and validation of BLE control frames (14-byte `B7` header); the rule for how commands transform the 11 values. Unrelated to network JSON |
| `wire.py` | Network frames: 4-byte big-endian length + strict UTF-8 JSON; limit 16384 bytes; a 5 s deadline for each read and write; `read_frame(..., idle_timeout=...)` (2026-10-15) waits that long for the first header byte, then 5 s for the rest of the frame; `ProtocolError` |
| `tls.py` | TLS client and server contexts; TLS 1.2 minimum; the host-name constant `TLS_SERVER_NAME`; no "do not verify" branch |
| `dummy_fixtures.json` | The editable 11-channel dummy-data table (4 rows: at rest, a chop-like row, a fast-rotation row and an int16-boundary row). The firmware header is generated from it |

**`firmware/esp32/`**

| File | What it does |
|---|---|
| `platformio.ini` | Six build environments: the default `firebeetle32` (protected, ID 1), `firebeetle32-left` (ID 1), `firebeetle32-right` (ID 2), `firebeetle32-left-glove` (ID 1) and `firebeetle32-right-glove` (ID 2) with Siqiang's module at 50 Hz, and the unprotected diagnostic `firebeetle32-unprotected-diagnostic`. `[env]` has `lib_ignore = glove_sensor`; only the `-glove` environments clear it |
| `src/main.cpp` | All the BLE peripheral run-time logic: the GATT service and its 7 characteristics, pairing security, the calls into the glove module (`gloveBegin` in `setup()`, since 2026-10-16 up to `kGloveBeginAttempts` tries through `comms::beginWithRetries`, lines 441-448; `gloveUpdate` every pass; `produceSample()` at each send tick, lines 404-431), sending samples at the set rate, the control queue, source statistics, the `glove_begin_failed`/`glove_begin`/`comms_ready`/`comms_stats` serial lines (the boot line `glove_begin` with `attempts`, lines 449-450), the `erase-bonds` serial command |
| `src/dummy_glove.cpp` | The built-in dummy glove module (2026-10-15; linked when `COMMS_GLOVE_MODULE` is 0): fixture rows as physical floats, and the optional IDLE/MOVING cycle |
| `src/glove_module.cpp` | For Siqiang's module (linked when `COMMS_GLOVE_MODULE` is 1): `gloveShouldSend()` = his `gloveIsMoving()`, or always true with `COMMS_GLOVE_SEND_ALL=1` (2026-10-15) |
| `include/comms_glove.h` | The glove module API (`gloveBegin`, `gloveUpdate`, `gloveRead`, `gloveShouldSend`; lines 53-71; `gloveBegin` must be safe to call again after `false`) and its build switches: `COMMS_GLOVE_MODULE`, `COMMS_DUMMY_MOVING_MS`, `COMMS_DUMMY_IDLE_MS`, `COMMS_GLOVE_SEND_ALL`; `kGloveGated` (lines 79-80) (2026-10-15). Since 2026-10-16 the boot retry (D38): `COMMS_GLOVE_BEGIN_ATTEMPTS` (default 3, 1 to 10) and `COMMS_GLOVE_BEGIN_RETRY_MS` (default 500, 0 to 10000), range-checked by `static_assert`; `kGloveBeginAttempts`, `kGloveBeginRetryMs`; the template `beginWithRetries(begin, pause, maxAttempts, attempts)`, which calls `begin` until it returns true or `maxAttempts` calls have failed, calls `pause(failedAttempt)` only between two calls, and reports the number of calls made |
| `include/comms_dummy_gate.h` | The portable parts of the dummy module, host-tested: the IDLE/MOVING cycle `dummyMoving` and `dummyFixtureSample` (2026-10-15) |
| `lib/glove_sensor/` | Unchanged copies of Siqiang's `glove_sensor.cpp`, `glove_sensor.h` and `movement_model_params.h` (in `src/`), and a README that says not to edit them. Made by `tools/sync_glove_module.py` (2026-10-15) |
| `movement_model_params.h` | A second copy of Siqiang's weights header, where his `#include "../../../movement_model_params.h"` finds it from `lib/glove_sensor/src/` (2026-10-15) |
| `include/comms_packet.h` | Version-3 constants (`kPacketVersion`, `kChannelCount`, `kPacketSize` = 38) and byte-by-byte serialisation of the packet; `serializePacket` takes exactly 11 values (a shorter array does not compile); `serializeFixturePacket` builds a packet from a dummy-table row, now only as the reference for the host tests (the board's dummy module gives the same bytes through the sensor path) |
| `include/comms_sample.h` | `scaleSample`: 11 physical floats to 11 int16 (rounding half away from zero; NaN/infinity refused; clamp by default or drop with `-DCOMMS_OUT_OF_RANGE_DROP=1`). Since 2026-10-15 also `prepareSample` (the outcome of one tick: send, unread, idle or refused), `SampleCounters`, and `sendTickDue` (the send tick that keeps its rate) |
| `include/comms_fixtures.h` | The dummy-data table generated from `common/dummy_fixtures.json` (`flash.py` rewrites it every time) |
| `include/comms_security.h` | Pure functions: authentication-bit checks, the notification gate, the sensor MTU floor of 41 (`sensorFitsMtu`, packet size + 3) and the control-write gate (`canAcceptControl`) |
| `include/comms_source_stats.h` | Sequence allocation, submission counters, serialisation of the 36-byte source statistics (marker `LCS3`, the gated flag, `idle`, `unread`, `refused`) |
| `include/comms_control.h` | The control state machine `ControlEngine`: commands (one version-3 packet in, 52-byte answer out), rate, file transfer, response cache. Also the control MTU floor of 64 (`controlFitsMtu`), `controlChunkCapacity` and the output floor `kControlMinOutput` = 52 |
| `include/comms_gatts_control.h` | Parses writes only on real WRITE events |
| `test/host_*.cpp` | Six host tests (packet, events, source statistics, control, sample scaling, and since 2026-10-15 the glove module switches and dummy module in `host_glove.cpp`, which since 2026-10-16 also tests `beginWithRetries` (`testBeginRetries`) and prints `begin_attempts` and `retry_ms` in its summary line; the scaling test is built once per out-of-range policy, the glove test once per set of switches), compiled and run with `g++` or `clang++`, whichever is on PATH; skipped when neither is found |

**`laptop/`**

| File | What it does |
|---|---|
| `bridge.py` | The full path for one glove: scan, connect, MTU and bond checks, bounded queue, TLS writes, ACK checks, metrics, tail handling at shutdown; `source_ticking_seconds` (first sensor subscription to the quiesce read) for the gated coverage rule |
| `dual_bridge.py` | Runs two uncoupled `Bridge` instances at once, with one shared observation window, and produces the combined report. This is what `demo.py` calls. `board_coverage` holds the coverage rules for gated and ungated boards; the progress line prints `gated=` |
| `source_audit.py` | Parses the 36-byte source statistics (`SOURCE_STATS_MAGIC = b"LCS3"`, `SOURCE_STATS_SIZE`, `SOURCE_FLAG_GATED`); reconciles "generated = received = ACKed"; reports `gated`, `source_idle`, `source_unread`, `source_refused`, `source_ticks` |
| `controls.py` | The BLE control channel: keyboard commands, rate, file transfer |
| `windows_pairing.py` | Pairs on Windows with "passkey entry + encrypted and authenticated", and checks the bond level |
| `ble_connection.py` | Turns off the Windows GATT cache when creating the Bleak client |
| `ble_parameters.py` | Above 10 Hz, asks Windows for throughput-optimised connection parameters and records the actual values |
| `ble_shutdown.py` | When a capture stops, first turns notifications off on the remote side while keeping the local callback, to collect the tail packets |
| `evidence.py` | Writes `packets.jsonl` and the logs in the background, bounded |
| `goodput.py` | Measures the rate of valid sensor bytes received |
| `reporting.py` | Exclusive placeholder and atomic write of report files; source fingerprints (`workspace_source_sha256`; since `4c6ab4c`, 2026-10-16, it also covers `firmware/esp32/lib` and `firmware/esp32/movement_model_params.h`, so a changed copy of Siqiang's module shows in the report) |
| `phone_simulator.py` | A desktop result subscriber, for rehearsal only. **It pushes the phone off when it connects** |
| `mtu_probe.py`, `ble_counter_receiver.py`, `bounded_telemetry_queue.py` | Early diagnostic tools, and a general-purpose queue the main path does not use |
| `requirements.txt` | `bleak==3.0.1` (the version must stay pinned: the shutdown tail protection depends on Bleak internals) |
| `tests/` | Tests for the three diagnostic tools above |

**`ultra96/`**

| File | What it does |
|---|---|
| `server.py` | The board service `CommsServer`: two loopback TLS listeners, the client limit and the eviction of the longest-silent ingest connection (`MAX_CLIENTS`, `INGEST_EVICTABLE_AFTER`, `idle_evicted`), the ingest idle limit (`INGEST_IDLE_TIMEOUT`, `--ingest-idle-timeout`), ingest de-duplication and ACK, the call into the model (`on_sample`, line 329) and the command echo, `_model_result` (identity and duplicate checks for model results), the single-subscriber result queue, the command-line entry (`--model`, `--model-path`, the `model_ready`/`model_failed` lines) |
| `model.py` | The model interface: `DummyModel`, `load_model`, `ModelPort` (calls `start`/`on_sample`, counts errors and slow calls, the thread-safe `emit`), `normalize_result` |
| `protocol.py` | Exact fields and value ranges of the five messages (version 3; subscription envelopes version 1), `session_id` rules, `result_id` generation, the label rule (`GESTURE_PATTERN`, `valid_gesture`) and the confidence rule (`valid_confidence`); `DUMMY_GESTURES` for the dummy model and command echoes |
| `diagnostics.py` | Optional bounded event log (`--event-log-dir`); accepts the events `model_result` and `model_result_refused` too |

**`ios-visualizer/`**

| File | What it does |
|---|---|
| `CommsNative/Package.swift` | Swift package definition; dependencies pinned to exact versions of SwiftNIO, NIOSSH, NIOSSL and swift-crypto (they need Swift tools 6.1 or newer); since 2026-10-15 it links swift-crypto's `Crypto` into `CommsBridge` on Linux only |
| `CommsNative/Sources/CommsCore/Protocol.swift` | Subscribe frame encoding, result validation (version 3, 10 keys, the label and confidence rules), `GestureResult.confidence` and `nextResultJSON`, stream framing (compiled and tested on Linux and macOS; built for iOS on 2026-10-16) |
| `CommsNative/Sources/CommsCore/DisplayState.swift` | Latest result, count, de-duplication (4096), 2 s expiry, and the per-result queue behind `CommsNextResult` (32 waiting, 2 s, `takeNext`; compiled and tested on Linux and macOS; built for iOS on 2026-10-16) |
| `CommsNative/Sources/CommsTransport/CommsClient.swift` | Building the two-hop SSH + TLS route, error classification (terminal or retry), back-off and reconnect |
| `CommsNative/Sources/CommsTransport/SSHRoute.swift` | Route parameters, port and timeout constants, failure types |
| `CommsNative/Sources/CommsTransport/Trust.swift` | Exact host-key comparison, password authentication, SSH channel byte conversion |
| `CommsNative/Sources/CommsTransport/Subscriber.swift` | Sends `SUBSCRIBE` after the TLS handshake, receives frames, frame deadline |
| `CommsNative/Sources/CommsTransport/Credentials.swift` | In-memory password holder that can be cleared |
| `CommsNative/Sources/CommsBridge/Configuration.swift` | Pinned host names, both SSH host public keys, CA fingerprint; CA import check; saving and loading the public settings and the CA (`ConfigurationStore`: saved into `Application Support/Comms`, or, since 2026-10-16 (`writableTarget()`), into the old `Week7` folder under the Week 7 names when `Comms` cannot be created for lack of permission and `Week7` is writable; loading reads the `Week7` files while the `Comms` ones are missing; 4.6). Imports CryptoKit and Security only where they exist (swift-crypto on Linux, where the Security certificate parse is skipped; 2026-10-15) |
| `CommsNative/Sources/CommsBridge/IntegrationController.swift` | Foreground session management, the connect button, disconnect on leaving the foreground. The session ID `ltc-comms` the client uses is set here (line 74) |
| `CommsNative/Sources/CommsBridge/SetupViewController.swift` | The setup form. Its text `session ltc-comms` (line 51) also names the session. When saving fails for a reason other than a form error, its message ends with the underlying error, `(domain code: description [underlying domain code])`, the bracket only when there is an underlying error, never the passwords (2026-10-16) |
| `CommsNative/Sources/CommsBridge/DisplayMailbox.swift` | The C functions exported to Unity, `CommsStart`, `CommsCopyDisplay`, `CommsStop`, `CommsNextResult` (compiled and tested on Linux and macOS; built for iOS and linked into the Unity build installed on the owner's iPhone on 2026-10-16, which does not call it), and the `Week7*` wrappers. The four lines of text are built here |
| `CommsNative/Tests/` | Swift tests: 72 ran on Linux in a Swift 6.1 container on 2026-10-15 (`PythonBoardTests` did not run there); 73, `PythonBoardTests` included, ran on macOS on the owner's Mac on 2026-10-16, and 76 (ConfigurationTests 9) after three settings-save tests were added to `CommsBridgeTests/ConfigurationTests.swift` that afternoon (`testSavesToCurrentDirectoryWhenItCanBeCreated`, `testSavesToLegacyDirectoryWhenParentIsNotWritable`, `testPermissionErrorWithoutLegacyDirectoryIsReported`; the last two skip themselves when run as root); the same day `TransportTests` and `TrustTests` (34) passed on the iOS Simulator and on the owner's iPhone (iOS 26.6.1), with the iOS test fixtures in `LocalPeers.swift` |
| `NativePreview/` | A preview app without Unity, and its UI tests |
| `tools/patch_export.py` | Week 7 receiver: patches the generated code of that one Unity export (fork only) |
| `tools/configure_xcode.py` | Links `CommsNative` into `UnityFramework` (fork only) |
| `verify-import.py`, `import-manifest.json` | Check the Unity export as first imported; only for a project not yet patched (fork only) |
| `reference/TlsDataReceiver.cs` | Archived copy of Jie Ling's original receiver script (fork only) |
| `xcode-export/` | The Unity export used in Week 7, 3479 files, large files in LFS (fork only) |
| `README.md`, `NATIVE-INTEGRATION.md` | Notes on the source and on the integration boundary |

**`phone/` (reference and diagnostics)**

| File | What it does |
|---|---|
| `receiver.py` | Standard-library Python receiver (Termux/iSH); connects to the local forwarded port 19999 |
| `unity/CommsPhoneCore.cs` | C# protocol, TLS trust and receive loop, with no Unity dependency (compiled at C# 7.0 on 2026-10-15). Its result queue is emptied at every new connection (4.6) |
| `unity/CommsPhoneReceiver.cs` | Android reference Unity component; the phone must open its own SSH local forward |
| `ish_control.py` | Temporary maintenance-channel script for the iSH route (that route is abandoned) |
| `tests/` | Tests for the Python above, and a C# self-test (`CoreSelfTest.cs`, run by `run_core_tests.ps1`; needs PowerShell 7; passed 113 checks under PowerShell 7.6.7 on 2026-10-15) |

**`tools/`**

| File | What it does |
|---|---|
| `ssh_tunnel.py` | Builds the two-hop, loopback-only OpenSSH local-forward command (explicit `ProxyCommand`, strict host keys) |
| `demo_service.py` | Inspects or starts the board service over SSH. `BOARD_ROOT`, the fallback `BOARD_SOURCE` and `board_source()` (the folder the newest local deployment record names) are here |
| `deploy_service.py` | Deploys the board service (2026-10-14): uploads `common/` and `ultra96/` into a new folder, optionally installs a renewed certificate, swaps the running service with checks, SIGKILL escalation and rollback; password prompts, strict `known_hosts`; the board's record in `BOARD_ROOT/evidence/active-deployment.json` and a local copy in `.comms-local/deployments/`. Since 2026-10-16 an SSH failure whose server offers no password login also prints a hint to connect the NUS VPN (6.5, D33). First real run on 2026-10-16 (6.1) |
| `check_board.py` | Checks one flashed board over real BLE against a service on the laptop (2026-10-14): source reconciliation, rate, commands, dummy-model results; the summary line also prints `gated`, `idle`, `unread`, `refused` (2026-10-15) |
| `export_capture.py` | Writes a capture's samples (`packets.jsonl`) as CSV in physical units, one row per sample (2026-10-15) |
| `sync_glove_module.py` | Copies Siqiang's module from `hardware/gloves/integration_files/` into `firmware/esp32/lib/glove_sensor/src/` and `firmware/esp32/movement_model_params.h`; `--check` only compares (2026-10-15) |
| `demo_boards.py` | Reads and writes the left/right Bluetooth address map (`.comms-local/boards.json`). The built-in default addresses are here |
| `generate_comms_pki.py` | Generates the CA and the server certificate. `--renew-server-from` renews only the server certificate with the existing CA |
| `generate_dummy_fixtures.py` | Generates the firmware header `comms_fixtures.h` from the JSON |
| `comms_demo.py` | Packet examples, a single-board send demo, offline log audit |
| `rehearse_comms.py` | Full rehearsal over local loopback (no SSH, no hardware) |
| `rehearse_remote_comms.py` | Remote rehearsal through an existing tunnel with both the ingest and the gateway forward (its synthetic source sends dummy-table rows; `--ble --address` relays one real board; the command in 11). **It pushes the phone's subscription off** |
| `video_evidence.py` | Extracts, from one capture, one example sample and its ACK per board; reads only version-3 captures |

**`tests/`**: 50 test files, 3 files under `fixtures/` that hold 2 golden vectors (`comms-golden.json` has two packet vectors; `comms-dummy.bin` and `comms-boundaries.bin` are their raw 38 bytes), and `linux/deploy_remote_test.sh` (the deploy tool's board-side checks, run in Linux or WSL only through `test_deploy_service.py` with `B07_LINUX_DEPLOY_TEST=1`). New on 2026-10-14: `test_model_interface.py` (the model interface and its server integration), `test_protocol_rules.py` (label and confidence rules, `physical_values`), `test_v3_events.py` (it replaced `test_v2_events.py`), `test_firmware_sample.py` (the scaling host test, both policies), `test_deploy_service.py`, `test_check_board.py`. New on 2026-10-15: `test_firmware_glove.py` (builds `host_glove.cpp` with several sets of glove switches, and checks that invalid switches do not compile), `test_export_capture.py`, `test_glove_module_sync.py` (the copies equal Siqiang's files; only the `-glove` environments link the module); `test_flash.py` gained the `--glove` case, `test_dual_bridge.py` the gated coverage cases, and `test_transport.py` the idle limit and eviction cases. On 2026-10-16 (`4c6ab4c`) `test_laptop_controls.py` gained a case that the report fingerprint covers the copies of the glove module, and later that day `test_deploy_service.py` gained `test_a_jump_host_without_password_login_points_at_the_vpn` (the deploy tool's VPN hint, 6.5 D33), and `test_firmware_glove.py` gained the case `external-one-try` (`-DCOMMS_GLOVE_BEGIN_ATTEMPTS=1 -DCOMMS_GLOVE_BEGIN_RETRY_MS=0`) and four invalid retry switches that must not compile (attempts 0 and 11, retry -1 and 10001), with `begin_attempts` and `retry_ms` in its expected summaries (13 tests; 6.5 D38). Files are named after what they test: `test_sensor.py` (packet format), `test_source_audit.py`, `test_laptop_controls.py`, `test_bridge_*.py`, `test_dual_*.py`, `test_transport*.py` (TLS, framing, certificate tool), `test_server_*.py`, `test_ssh_trust.py`, `test_demo_*.py`, `test_firmware_*.py` (compile and run the firmware host tests), `test_rehearsal.py`, and others.

### 12.4 Git and local-only directories

| Location | Contents |

### 12.6 What to change, and in which file

| What to change | File |
|---|---|
| How many values a packet holds, packet length | `firmware/esp32/include/comms_packet.h` (`kChannelCount`), `common/sensor.py` (`CHANNELS`, `SCALES`); `firmware/esp32/include/comms_sample.h` (`kChannelScale`, one scale per channel; a channel without an entry would get scale 0) and `firmware/esp32/test/host_sample.cpp` (asserts 11 channels); the `values` contract of `on_sample` (11 floats; `ultra96/model.py`, 7.2); the MTU floors, the command frame size and the golden vectors follow, and their tests must be updated; `comms_source_stats.h` and `laptop/source_audit.py` for the statistics marker; `tools/generate_dummy_fixtures.py` and `common/dummy_fixtures.json` |
| Scales or the out-of-range policy | `firmware/esp32/include/comms_sample.h` and `common/sensor.py` `SCALES` (both together); the policy is the build flag `COMMS_OUT_OF_RANGE_DROP` |
| Where the firmware gets samples | The glove module behind `firmware/esp32/include/comms_glove.h`: `src/dummy_glove.cpp` (the built-in dummy) or Siqiang's module (`-glove` environments); the tick itself is `produceSample()` in `src/main.cpp` |
| How often the firmware tries `gloveBegin()` at boot | `COMMS_GLOVE_BEGIN_ATTEMPTS` and `COMMS_GLOVE_BEGIN_RETRY_MS` in `firmware/esp32/include/comms_glove.h` (build flags; D38) |
| When a glove sends (gating) | Siqiang's detector decides (his module). On the Communications side: `src/glove_module.cpp` (`gloveShouldSend`), the switches in `comms_glove.h` (`COMMS_GLOVE_SEND_ALL`; for the dummy, `COMMS_DUMMY_MOVING_MS` and `COMMS_DUMMY_IDLE_MS`) |
| Siqiang's module changed in the team repo | Merge the team main, then `python -m tools.sync_glove_module`; never edit the copies in `firmware/esp32/lib/glove_sensor/` or `firmware/esp32/movement_model_params.h` |
| Source statistics fields | `firmware/esp32/include/comms_source_stats.h` and `laptop/source_audit.py` together (the length is also the firmware check, 4.2); the fake peripherals in the tests serve the same 36 bytes |
| Coverage rule of a gated board | `board_coverage` in `laptop/dual_bridge.py` |
| Ingest idle limit, client slots | `ultra96/server.py` (`INGEST_IDLE_TIMEOUT`, `INGEST_EVICTABLE_AFTER`, `MAX_CLIENTS`), or at start-up `--ingest-idle-timeout` |
| Default send rate | `platformio.ini` (`-DCOMMS_INITIAL_RATE_HZ`; 50 in the `-glove` environments) or, at run time, `demo.py run --rate` |
| How the board produces results | The model: `--model MODULE` on the service; the interface in `ultra96/model.py`; the dummy model is `DummyModel` there |
| Which labels and confidence values are allowed | `ultra96/protocol.py` (also used by `ultra96/model.py`), `CommsCore/Protocol.swift`, `phone/receiver.py`, `phone/unity/CommsPhoneCore.cs` (all four change together, then the phone app is rebuilt) |
| `CommsNextResult` limits (32 waiting, 2 s) | `DisplayState.swift` (`pendingCapacity`, `pendingLifetime`) |
| Text or functions for Unity | `ios-visualizer/CommsNative/Sources/CommsBridge/DisplayMailbox.swift` |
| Session ID | Laptop: `--session-id` or `LTC_COMMS_SESSION`. Board: the service's `--session-id`. Phone: two places that change together, `IntegrationController.swift:74` (the value used) and the form text in `SetupViewController.swift:51`; then rebuild |
| Jump-host and board host names, SSH host public keys, CA fingerprint | Laptop: `tools/ssh_tunnel.py` and the `demo.py` arguments. Phone: `Configuration.swift` (rebuild) |
| Board deployment directory | `BOARD_ROOT` and the fallback `BOARD_SOURCE` in `tools/demo_service.py` (also used by `tools/deploy_service.py`); after a deploy, `.comms-local/deployments/` |
| Bluetooth addresses of the two boards | Run `python flash.py`; the result is in `.comms-local/boards.json` |
| Queue length, freshness, timeouts, ACK window | Laptop: `BridgeConfig` in `laptop/bridge.py`, and `demo.py`. Board: `ultra96/server.py`. Phone: `SSHRoute.swift`, `DisplayState.swift` |
| Certificates | `tools/generate_comms_pki.py` |

## 13. Not yet confirmed

- Whether two gated boards pass the gated coverage rule together through the Ultra96. (Two ungated boards at 50 Hz through the Ultra96, with the phone receiving all 6035 dummy-model results, about 100 per second, passed on 2026-10-17; 6.1.)
- Whether a long run (600 s or more) on version 3 through the Ultra96 stays clean.
- Range: never tested.
- Whether Siqiang's module works in the Communications firmware on his gloves (calibration, the MOVING timing, 50 Hz with BLE running). On the owner's board without sensors only its failure path ran (2026-10-16).
- Which WHO_AM_I value each glove's IMU reports (7.1, point 5; a question for Siqiang). Any value other than `0x70` makes `gloveBegin()` fail on every try (the retry of D38 does not change that), and the glove sends nothing.
- Whether the `gloveBegin()` retry (D38) recovers a real glove whose first try fails and a later one succeeds: on hardware it retried only on a board without sensors, where every try fails (the plain build succeeded at the first try); the host test covers the path where a later try succeeds.
- Whether Wen Ree's model can run inside the board service process (`/usr/bin/python3`, user `xilinx`, no sudo) or must be a separate process (7.2).
- Whether `python demo.py service --start` restarts the deployed service correctly from the first deployment record (`deployment-20261016T050629Z.json`): built with unit tests only, not run against the real board (6.5, D16).
- The deploy tool's rollback, SIGKILL and refusal paths on the real board (they ran only on Linux; the deploy of 2026-10-16 did not need them), and the eviction of the longest-silent ingest connection on the real board.
- Why the app container on the owner's iPhone has a root-owned `Library/Application Support` (uid 0, gid 501, mode 0755, dated 2026-09-21; the container dates from 2026-09-21, and a containermanagerd data-protection mark is on the files). Not caused by anything done on 2026-10-16; the client works around it (4.6, 9.2).
- Whether the settings-save fix and its three tests pass on Linux and in the iOS Simulator: they ran only on macOS (76 tests, on `main` and again on the branch of pull request #20), and the fix ran in the app on the owner's iPhone (4.6, 6.2).
- Whether `CommsPhoneReceiver.cs` compiles in Unity (checked by reading only; `CommsPhoneCore.cs` compiles at C# 7.0 and its self-test passes).
- Whether the team main `be97a04` passes its Python tests: none is recorded in a checkout of it after the merges of pull requests #19 and #20 on 2026-10-16. Its Python, firmware and tool code equals that of `44d0f71` (824 passed, 4 skipped there, on this laptop). Its tree equals that of `01b25de`, the commit of pull request #20, on which `swift test` passed 76 tests on the Mac; the iOS Simulator tests (34 + 1) ran on the package before the settings-save fix (6.2). The fork's `main` contains `7d8317a` and, since `c2fa1f6`, `be97a04` (6.2). On this laptop it gave 843 passed, 4 skipped at `8923a59` (the deploy-tool change, before `be97a04` was merged), and 848 passed, 4 skipped with the `gloveBegin()` retry on top of `8923a59`, before that commit was rebased onto `c2fa1f6`; no full run is recorded on `c2fa1f6` or on the rebased commit (6.2).
- `demo.py`'s `Phone expected increase` and the rehearsal tools' exact sample-to-result match apply only while the dummy model runs. With a real model, Communications' check is the board's `result_write_complete` count against the phone's `Received` increase (D39); the tool that tallies it does not exist yet (below).
- Whether `configure_xcode.py` works on a new Unity export. (On the existing export it ran on 2026-10-16 and changed nothing, because the export was already configured.)
- Direct mode (the phone not going through the jump host) was never verified on site. V1 does not use it.
- Why the phone missed 109 results on 2026-09-21.
- Wen Ree's and Jie Ling's replies about the interfaces in section 7 (Siqiang answered and delivered on 2026-10-15).

The owner's rules (2026-10-14):

- Only the newest version is supported. No interoperability between versions; upgrading means reflashing, redeploying and reinstalling everything together (8, step 1).
- Teammates deliver code by pushing it to the team repo on GitHub, not over Telegram.

The owner's direction (2026-10-14), proposed to the teammates and final when they confirm (3.1, 7, 8):

- The model gets the 11 values as physical floats. Transport stays int16.
- The game gets results one at a time through a structured per-result function, `CommsNextResult` (one compact JSON object per call; bytes written, 0, or the negative of the bytes needed including the terminating 0; 512-byte buffer; called in a loop each frame). Written on 2026-10-14; compiled and tested on Linux on 2026-10-15; tested on macOS and built for iOS on 2026-10-16, and in the team main since then (`7d8317a`); installed the same day on the owner's iPhone in the rebuilt Week 7 app, which does not call it.
- Jie Ling does everything in Unity and the iOS build (the C# calls, the game logic, the export, adding `CommsNative`, building and installing on their own iPhone with their own Mac); olsonwangyj pushes `CommsNative` to the team repo and writes the steps for adding it. `CommsNative` is in the team main since 2026-10-16 (`7d8317a`), with the settings-save fix since `be97a04` the same day; the steps are not written yet (8 step 7).
- The real-data packet is protocol version 3; 38 bytes with the 11 values only; the scales are checked against Siqiang's real output before they are fixed. Implemented on 2026-10-14 with this layout.
- The model interface on the board as in 7.2 (`start(emit)`, `on_sample`, thread-safe `emit`), loaded with `--model`. Implemented on 2026-10-14; the names change if Wen Ree picks others, and a separate-process model would need a bridge between the two processes on the board.

Settled with Siqiang on 2026-10-15 (his answers and his delivered module, 2.3, 7.1): a glove sends samples only while his detector says MOVING, and the Communications firmware calls his decision for each sample (the owner's request). So the IDLE/MOVING state does not travel in the packet, and the 11-value layout stays.

Decided by the agent within Communications on 2026-10-14 (6.5), open to the owner's review: the subscription envelopes stay at version 1; `request_id` stays; the statistics marker becomes `LCS3`; the label rule (1 to 32 of `A–Z a–z 0–9 _ -`) and the confidence rule (finite, 0 to 1); commands bypass the model; the dummy model gives one result per sample; confidence is passed on unrounded; the `CommsNextResult` counters reset on a new Connect.

Decided by the agent within Communications on 2026-10-15 (6.5, D20 to D31), open to the owner's review: the gate is called once per sample; a tick that sends nothing takes no `seq`; idle, unread and refused ticks are counted in the source statistics, which grow to 36 bytes under the same marker `LCS3`, while `clamped` and `race_dropped` stay on the serial line only; the send-all switch for recording; the gated coverage rule; the 600 s ingest idle limit with eviction; Siqiang's module built from checked copies, with the weights header in both locations; the `-glove` environments at 50 Hz; `flash.py --glove`; the capture exporter; the Swift Linux build fix.

Decided by the agent within Communications on 2026-10-16 (6.5, D32), open to the owner's review: the Unity export's `Package.resolved` keeps the package's tested pins (swift-asn1 1.7.2, swift-collections 1.6.0), with the origin hash Xcode 26.2 computes for the current graph, instead of the 1.7.3 versions Xcode floated to (9.2).

Decided by the agent within Communications later on 2026-10-16 (6.5, D33 to D36), open to the owner's review: the deploy tool points at the NUS VPN when the jump host offers no password login; the remote rehearsal uses one OpenSSH tunnel with both forwards; one rehearsal or probe runs against the board at a time; local `main` was reset onto `origin/main` instead of keeping a redundant local merge of the team main.

Decided by the agent within Communications on the afternoon of 2026-10-16 (6.5, D37), open to the owner's review: when `Application Support/Comms` cannot be created for lack of permission and the old `Week7` folder is writable, the phone client saves its settings and CA there under the Week 7 names, and the form's save message shows the underlying error of any other failure.

Decided by Claude, the agent, at the owner's request on 2026-10-16 (6.5, D38), open to the owner's review: the firmware retries `gloveBegin()` up to three times at boot, 500 ms apart, as a Communications-side mitigation of 7.1 point 3; the root cause stays in Siqiang's module.

The owner's decision (2026-10-16; 6.5, D39): Communications passes when the data is delivered: every sample a glove sends is received and acknowledged (generated = received = acknowledged per glove), and every result the model emits on the board reaches the phone unchanged. The number of results, their labels, confidence and accuracy, and the game's actions are not Communications' criterion.

Still open:

- Out-of-range values: clamp or drop that one sample, to be agreed with Wen Ree (the firmware does clamp by default and can be built to drop); and whether the model needs a per-sample flag (8, step 1).
- The scales: checked against Siqiang's real samples before the layout is frozen (7.1).
- Whether the MOVING onset lag (at best about 0.2 to 0.4 s, longer for gentle movements; the exact lags need real data), the still tail after each movement and one-glove windows suit the model (Wen Ree; any change is on the detector side, with Siqiang; 7.2).
- Whether Siqiang changes the points found in his module (7.1): 1, the include path; 2, the classifier reset after a failed reading; 3, one failed calibration read failing `gloveBegin()` (mitigated in Communications by the retry, D38; the root cause is his); 5, the strict WHO_AM_I check (with the question which value each glove prints). Point 4 is a property for Wen Ree. The owner decides whether to raise them.
- The label set and the result rate (Wen Ree and Jie Ling). Communications' own pass criterion no longer waits for them (D39).
- The delivery tally for real-model runs: a Communications tool that reads the board's `model_result`, `result_write_complete` and drop events after a run, for comparison with the phone's `Received` increase (planned as an option of `python demo.py service`), before the Monday 10-19 dry run (D39; 8, step 6).
- The `CommsNextResult` queue limits: provisional 32 waiting and 2 s after reaching the phone, revisited once Wen Ree says how often results come.
